# Language maturity

A per-language reliability reference: **is a language's output trustworthy, and what work (if any) is outstanding?**
This exists because the single most visible number — the referee-corroboration % in
`test/referee-eval.test.ts` — is a **confounded** signal and a poor proxy for maturity on its own.

## How to read this (why the referee % ≠ maturity)

The referee floors measure folded agreement with an **independent** transcription source (wikipron / epitran /
kaikki). That number is deflated by two things that have nothing to do with our engine:

- **Referee quality.** wikipron/kaikki dumps are noisy: proper nouns, letter-name rows (`A → eɪ`), acronyms,
  taxonomic Latin, and cross-dialect variants (rhotic vs non-rhotic, N-vs-S Welsh, 3-dialect Irish). English
  measures 36% almost entirely for this reason, not because the engine is 64% wrong.
- **Fold ceiling.** We deliberately fold away layers where we are *richer* than the broad referee (length, tone,
  offglide notation) — so real distinctions we get right, and real ones we get *wrong*, can both be invisible to
  the number (Welsh's n/r/l length errors are folded away, for instance).

So a low number can mean a noisy referee, a fold ceiling, **or** genuine work — you tell them apart by the
**residual composition**, not the headline:

- A **systematic segment class** in the residual (e.g. Welsh's `ɨ ≠ i`) → real, fixable work.
- **Diffuse** proper-noun / notation / dialect noise → referee artifact, not a backlog.

Maturity here is therefore judged on **two axes**, read together with the per-language unit test (the committed
correctness anchor):

1. **Core reliability** — is the segmental g2p correct for the input it supports?
2. **Scope / completeness** — are whole subsystems (text front-end, pitch/tone, diacritization, number
   compositor) deferred, narrowing the input it's reliable *for*?

### Fold-masking coefficient — when the folded % overstates (the `*` marker)

The fold ceiling cuts both ways. Most folds are harmless: a **one-to-one relabel** (ɾ→r) just renames, and a
**suprasegmental strip** (tone, stress) peels off a layer we render richer than the referee. Neither can turn a
*wrong* g2p output into a match. But a **collapse fold** — one that merges ≥2 phonemically distinct sounds into one
target (`[e o ɒ]→a`) — *can*: if the true vowel is /e/ and the g2p emits /o/, folding both to [a] scores it correct.
When the collapsed contrast is **unrecoverable from the input** (the short/majhūl vowels an undiacritized abjad
doesn't write), the folded % is then measuring a coin-flip as a hit, and **overstates**.

`tools/referee-eval/masking-coefficient.ts` auto-tags every fold (`relabel` / `suprasegmental` / `strip` /
`structural` / `collapse`) from its shape and **measures** the masking coefficient: re-run the eval keeping every
fold *except* the collapse ones, and report the drop. Shape alone can't tell a benign **reduction** collapse
(Bulgarian unstressed `[a ɐ ɤ]→a`, +86pp — but 99.9% symbol) from a masking one, so the discriminator is **symbol
accuracy**: reduction leaves the segments intact (99%+), a real-contrast collapse cannot. The `*` below flags rows
where **masking-lift ≥ 10pp and symbol accuracy < 92%** — the folded headline leans on a collapse of a
genuinely-contrastive, input-underdetermined axis, so read the **symbol accuracy** (and the verdict) as the truer
figure, not the folded %. (Numbers are the tool's, reproducible at HEAD — the masking-lift is a *delta*, so it is
robust to a language row's headline drifting.)

| lang | masking-lift | symbol | verdict | collapsed contrast |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **fa** Persian | +38.5pp | 90.7% | 🟡 | `[e o ɒ]→a` — the 3-way short vowel, unwritten in the abjad |
| **ur** Urdu | +24.5pp | 86.9% | 🟡 | majhūl `[o ɔ]→u`, `[e ɛ]→i` |
| **skr** Saraiki | +19.5pp | 91.7% | 🔷 | majhūl `[o ɔ]→u`, `[e ɛ]→i` |
| **ps** Pashto | +20.1pp | 80.5% | 🟡 | `[a i u ɪ ʊ]→ə` — all short vowels to schwa |
| **sd** Sindhi | +14.7pp | 91.4% | 🟡 | majhūl `[o ɔ]→u`, `[e ɛ]→i` |
| **bn** Bengali | +11.3pp | 87.6% | ✅ | `[æ ɛ]→e` (the এ/এ্যা ambiguity); rule-eval only — the ✅ rests on the neural OOV tier |

All are already flagged 🟡/🔷 (or, for bn, ✅ on a *different* path) — the masking coefficient doesn't change a
verdict, it explains *why the folded number reads higher than the language's honest accuracy on its hardest axis*
(fa's row already reports a conservative, masking-discounted headline for exactly this reason). Just below the cut:
`mn` (+8.3pp / 87.0% — real uvular/velar `[q ɡ ɢ]→k` masking, but sub-threshold magnitude) and `lb`/`oc`/`ca` (high
lift but ≥92% symbol → benign reduction, correctly *not* flagged).

## Verdict legend

| | Meaning |
|---|---|
| ✅ **Reliable** | Trust the output. Deferrals are minor/notation, or the low referee % is just referee noise / a fold ceiling. |
| 🟢 **Reliable · bounded** | Trust the output for the input the orthography determines. A bounded, documented class is **underdetermined by the writing system itself** (an unwritten contrast — glottal stop, stress, ⟨e⟩/schwa) — closed by a lexicon for attested words, with an irreducible OOV tail. Unlike 🟡, this is **not outstanding work**: there is no path, because the distinguishing information is not in the input. A characterized ceiling, not a backlog item. |
| 🟡 **Reliable + lexical tail** | Correct for the bulk; a documented exception/lexicon layer would close a small, *specific* class (open/close mid vowels, loanwords, stress on learned words). Safe, with a known small error rate on those classes. |
| 🟠 **Scope-limited** | Core g2p correct, but a whole subsystem is deferred — reliable only for a *narrower* input (diacritized Arabic; pinyin, not raw Hanzi; segmental, without pitch/tone). |
| 🔵 **In active development** | Not yet at a stable plateau; the bring-up is still adding a core layer. |
| 🔷 **Single-source verified** | Trustworthy, but corroborated against ONE substantial authoritative source/tradition — no independent second source to triangulate. Reliable within that tradition; the caveat is evidence *breadth* (a systematic error shared with the source would go undetected), not output quality. Distinct from ⛔ (no substantial independent attestation) and from ✅/🟢/🟡 (which rest on an *independent* referee). |
| ⛔ **Cannot-verify** | Examined but NOT independently verifiable — no substantial independent attestation exists, and the language is a near-clone of a covered one, so a clone engine and a clone referee agree trivially. Correctness on the shared bulk is asserted, not measured; only the distinctive features are gold-checked. |

**🟡 vs 🟢 — the discriminator:** ask whether the input contains the information needed for the correct output. If the fix is missing code/data (the info *is* in the input, we just haven't written the rule/lexicon) → **🟡** (a path exists). If the input *under-determines* the output (the writing system doesn't encode the contrast) → **🟢** (capped — a lexicon memorises attested words, OOV is a principled coin-flip forever). Note 🟢 is a *word-level* judgment: a homograph resolvable by sentence context (English *read*/*read* via POS) is **not** 🟢, because the disambiguating info *is* in the input at the clause level — Tagalog's final-ʔ (*bata* child [bataʔ] vs robe [bata]) isn't recoverable even from context, only from the word's lexical identity, which is what makes it genuinely capped.

**⛔ vs 🔷 — the discriminator:** is there a large, reliable body of attestation? If the only "source" is circular — a clone of a covered language, or self-referential — → **⛔** (cannot verify; a clone engine and a clone referee agree trivially, e.g. bho). If there's a substantial authoritative source but only ONE tradition/vertical (no *independent* second source to triangulate) → **🔷** (single-source verified — e.g. Wu, corroborated against the 101k-entry Wugniu/Zaonhe tradition). The difference is the volume and quality of attestation, not merely "referee vs no referee."

**Scope — the FIRST gate (before any of the above):** does the language have a **community-adopted written form** to phonemise? A G2P consumes an orthography, so a language must *have* one that its own community uses. Two exclusions follow. (1) A *variety/accent of a covered language* is out of scope — it has no separate ISO code and no separate orthography; it would be an accent-transfer on the parent (Cockney/AAVE/Scots-accent → `en`; not catalogued). (2) A *distinct language with no community-adopted orthography* is also out of scope — choosing a script and a phonology *for* it is prescriptive, not descriptive: it takes a side in the community's own unsettled writing/identity question, flattens dialectal variation, and lends false authority to one made-up standard. **Chittagonian (ctg) was built and then removed on this rule** — a distinct ~13M-speaker language, but primarily *spoken*, with three competing scripts (Bengali/Latin/Arabic) and no adopted standard; producing "Chittagonian canonical IPA" would have imposed the standardisation the community declined. (Contrast the single-source Hindi-belt lects bho/awa/hne, which *do* have a community-adopted Devanagari tradition — Awadhi's *Ramcharitmanas*, Bhojpuri's press, Chhattisgarhi's official status — so phonemising them *follows* an existing practice rather than inventing one.) The test is legitimacy (is it written by its community?), not utility (would many people use it?).

## The languages

Sorted alphabetically by language code. Columns: **Referee** = primary measured folded-agreement (floor in
parens); **2nd** = an independent secondary source where one exists (a much better quality signal than a noisy
primary — see fr/pt/de/ru/ha/vi/tr); **Verdict** = the ✅/🟢/🟡/🔷/⛔ status; the last column is the two-line
orientation — *what makes this language's g2p interesting, and what (if anything) is left to do*.

| Lang | Referee (floor) | 2nd source | Verdict | What's notable · what's outstanding |
|------|-----------------|-----------|---------|--------------------------|
| **acm** Iraqi Arabic | 18.5% (.16) | — | 🟡 | **Arabic dialect (Baghdadi *gilit*, ~15M).** Same architecture as Egyptian (`arz`): a one-file shift table over the MSA g2p output. Its fingerprint is the gilit reflex the dialect is *named* for (qultu→gilit): ق→[ɡ], while it stays conservative everywhere else — ج kept [d͡ʒ], خ kept [x], interdentals ث/ذ/ظ kept. **Outstanding:** the vowels — Iraqi short-vowel reduction/epenthesis is a wholesale restructuring the MSA diacritizer can't produce (the arz/apc data-blocked wall), on a small 108-word referee. Consonants done. |
| **acw** Hijazi Arabic | 42.5% (.38) | — | 🔷 | **Arabic dialect (Hijazi — western Saudi, Mecca/Medina, ~11M).** A one-file VarietyDef shift table on the MSA g2p output. Its distinctive profile pairs the voiced **ق→[ɡ]** with a **RETAINED affricate ج=[d͡ʒ]** (unlike Egyptian [ɡ] / Levantine [ʒ]) and keeps **خ=[x]** (not the uvular [χ] of Gulf/Libyan); interdentals merge (ث→t, ذ→d, ظ→[zˤ]), diphthongs monophthongize. Verified against wikipron acw_arab_broad (HUMAN, 1891) — 42.5% folded, the **arz-range high end** of the family. **Outstanding:** the shared short-vowel restructuring gap (the MSA diacritizer restores MSA vowels; the % is a vowel-tail artifact, consonants + diphthongs exact). |
| **af** Afrikaans | 79.5% folded (.79) | Wiktionary + RCRL | ✅ | **Indo-European (West Germanic, daughter of Dutch), South Africa/Namibia (~7M), Latin + diacritics.** **FOUR TIERS**, in precedence order: a 44-entry curated proper-noun lexicon → a **25,112-entry pronunciation lexicon** (RCRL, CC BY-SA 2.5 ZA) → an optional **neural OOV tagger** (async path only) → the rule engine. **The rule engine** is a greedy digraph-first g2p + the Germanic **open/closed-syllable vowel-length rule** (a single vowel is LONG/tense in an open syllable V.CV — water→vɑːtər — and SHORT/lax in a closed one VC#/VCC — kat→kat; engine lookahead), extended to the **unstressed-open** cell, where the vowel keeps the tense quality but takes it short (kilometer→kilumiətər). Obstruent **devoicing** is positional (hond→ɦɔnt), **regressive** before any voiceless obstruent (advies→atfis), and **blocked by a resyllabifying suffix**, since a coda rule cannot reach an onset (send·ing→sɛndəŋ); a seam ⟨d⟩ **degeminates** against /t d/ (veld·tog→fɛltɔχ, land·dros→landrɔs — the onset survives with its own voicing). Compounds are decomposed (shared core/germanicMorphology.ts) so each element carries its own stress and coda. Stress is first-syllable past the unstressed prefixes (be/ge/ver/ont/her) with a **derived suffix→syllables-from-end table**. Signatures: **⟨g⟩=[χ]** velar/uvular fricative (not [ɡ]), **⟨v⟩=[f]**, **⟨h⟩=[ɦ]**; ⟨w⟩ is **[v], but the glide [w] in a morpheme-initial obstruent cluster** (swaar→swɑːr, twee→twiə, verdwyn→fərdwəin, against antwoord→antvuərt across a syllable boundary); the long mids are **centering diphthongs** ⟨ee⟩/open-e=[iə], ⟨oo⟩/open-o=[uə] (een→iən, groot→χruət); ⟨y⟩=[əi], ⟨ui⟩=[œy], diminutive ⟨tjie⟩=[ki]. **TWO INDEPENDENT REFEREES**: en.wiktionary (2,220, human) and the RCRL Afrikaans Pronunciation Dictionary (27,428, CTexT/NWU — speech-technology lineage, no Wiktionary ancestry). Rules alone score **79.5% folded / 94.7% symbol** and **65.2% / 93.9%** respectively. ⚠ **Both referees are DICTIONARY-shaped and over-sample rare Latinate words: frequency-weighted, the rules read 96.2% / 92.9% of real-text TOKENS** — the word-exact figures understate what users hear by ~17–28pp, and the frequency-weighted number is floored as the regression guard. The **shipped** path (lexicons + rules) scores **86.1% against the independent primary, with zero regressions** — **86.0% with the 44 curated rows excluded**, since those are transcribed from that primary, so the circularity there is 0.1pp; the eval deliberately scores `phonemizeWordRules` alone, since both lexicons are built from a referee. The **tagger** (per-grapheme BiLSTM, 2.2 MB int8, 32,544 vetted training pairs = RCRL ∪ NCHLT-inlang) reads **91.4% word-exact / 98.7% symbol** on 3,873 dictionary-gold held-out words, against the rule engine's **63.5% / 93.5%** — the OOV tail is long, rare and Latinate, exactly where rules are weakest. **FOLDED:** stress and syllable dots are not emitted; nasalization is deferred (the visible n-deletion class is ×2/2220, and the flagship afrikɑ̃ːs rides in the curated lexicon). **Verdict — ✅, on the token-weighted residual measured against the INDEPENDENT PRIMARY.** ⚠ It must be measured there and not against the secondary: the 25,112-entry lexicon is built FROM the secondary and covers 25,112 of its 27,428 headwords, so scoring shipped output against it is scoring the answer key. Against en.wiktionary, which no lexicon tier is derived from except the 44 curated rows, the shipped residual is **2.23% of running-text tokens**, and it splits: **2.06% (126 types) is a TWO-SOURCE CONFLICT** — we emit the secondary's value and the primary disagrees (ander, baard, baie: the primary's inconsistent final devoicing, documented as not-to-be-chased since bring-up) — leaving **0.17% (168 types) of our own error**, loans and reduction on rare polysyllables. **Our genuine error is ~0.2% of real-text tokens.** ⚠ The low word-exact figures are dictionary-shape bias, not quality: both referees over-sample rare Latinate words, and the same engine is 96.2% / 92.9% frequency-weighted. **Stress is closed where it ships:** a perfect-stress oracle moves the shipped path by **−1 word against the primary** (which can only cover 529 of its rows) and **+28 (0.10pp) against the secondary** — the often-quoted 4.3pp is the RULE ENGINE's headroom, and the rules serve only 2,314 of 27,428 dictionary words. NOT 🟢: the OOV tagger reads held-out words at 91.4%, so the unwritten stress contrast is largely recoverable from spelling rather than "a principled coin-flip forever". NOT 🟡: there is no specific class a further lexicon or rule layer would close — the layers are built, and distilling the tagger into the sync path was measured and REJECTED (on frequent OOV traffic the rules are 97.4% against the tagger's 97.2%). **The binding constraint is data, not method:** ~32.6k pairs is the ceiling for this language — the three open dictionaries (RCRL, NCHLT-inlang, Lwazi) are one NWU/CSIR lineage and Lwazi adds zero headwords. Deferred, both minor: nasalization (visible n-deletion ×2/2220; the flagship afrikɑ̃ːs rides in the curated lexicon) and the ⟨êre⟩ suffix class (48 words at 93.75%, just under the derivation's 0.95 precision threshold; ≈0 shipped impact since all are lexicon-served). Method notes and every measurement: docs/afrikaans_stress_investigation.md.
| **afb** Gulf Arabic | 3.3% (.02) | — | 🟡 | **Arabic dialect (Khaleeji, ~15M).** One-file shift table on the MSA g2p output: ق→[ɡ], خ→[χ] (uvular), ج + interdentals kept. **The family's lowest referee %, and honestly so** — Gulf reduces short vowels to schwa [ə] pervasively (radd→rəd) and realizes final ة as [ə], so it diverges from the MSA vowels maximally even though the comparable consonants all match; short 2–3-phone words make one vowel mismatch fail the whole word. The % is a referee-vs-vowel-tail artifact, not a quality signal. **Outstanding:** the (deepest-in-family) data-blocked vowels. |
| **ajp** S. Levantine Arabic | 38.6% (.35) | — | 🔷 | **Arabic dialect (South Levantine — Palestinian/Jordanian, ~10M) — the southern sibling of North Levantine (apc).** A one-file VarietyDef on the MSA g2p output: ق→[ʔ], ج→[ʒ], ث/ذ→[t]/[d], diphthongs ay→eː/aw→oː. The single split from apc: **ظ→[zˤ]** (a SIBILANT, vs apc's stop [dˤ]) while ض stays [dˤ], keeping the two emphatics contrastive. Verified against wikipron ajp_arab_broad (HUMAN, 2513) — 38.6% folded, **above the apc sibling (23%)** and at the family high end. **Outstanding:** the shared short-vowel restructuring gap (consonants + diphthongs exact; the % is the family-wide vowel-tail artifact). |
| **ak** Akan (Twi) | single-source (kaikki 70%, 20w) | gold (test/akan.test.ts) | 🔷 | **Akan/Twi — Kwa (Niger-Congo), Ghana (~20M incl. L2).** Unlike Rajasthani, `ak` (a macrolanguage over Asante/Akuapem Twi + Fante) has a genuine community-adopted standardised Latin orthography (Bureau of Ghana Languages) → clears the scope gate; the reference is Asante/Akuapem Twi. A shallow rule g2p authored from **Dolphyne (1988) / Paster (2010)**. The signature is the **consonant DIGRAPH system**: a palatal series ⟨ky gy hy ny⟩ → t͡ɕ d͡ʑ ɕ ɲ and a **labialised series** ⟨tw dw kw gw hw nw⟩ → t͡ɕʷ d͡ʑʷ kʷ ɡʷ ɕʷ ŋʷ (the famous Akan labial-palatalisation: twi→t͡ɕʷi, akwaaba→akʷaaba), plus **Glide Formation** (a round vowel before a *different* vowel → w: boa→bwa), the nasal rules (coda **Place Assimilation** nkran→ŋkran + **Labial Nasalization** /b/→[m] after a nasal, mba→mma; Paster 2010), and **ATR harmony** (the orthography merges [e]/[ɪ]→⟨e⟩ and [o]/[ʊ]→⟨o⟩; recovered by spreading from the ⟨i u⟩/⟨ɛ ɔ⟩ triggers — kyerɛ→t͡ɕɪrɛ). **TONE (H/L) + vowel nasality** are lexical (Akan tone is unpredictable from the toneless orthography) → carried the **Romanian-stress way**: a mined lexicon (from the tone-marked kaikki + Paster) emits Chao letters on the shipped path (papa→pa˩pa˥, mifi→mĩ˩fi˥), with a tone-free rule path for the non-circular eval. Plus **numbers** (Twi cardinals). **Referee is thin** — no wikipron/epitran Akan, only a small kaikki set → 70% folded (segmental); anchor is the adjudicated gold → **🔷 single-source**. **Outstanding:** tone/nasality COVERAGE is small (~21 words — the ceiling is data volume, not method; no large toned Akan corpus exists), and the deep grammatical-tone rules (Tonal Plateauing across morphemes) await morphological parsing. |
| **ab** Abkhaz | 82.5%* (.93) | kaikki 65.5% (.93 sym) | 🔷 | **A NORTHWEST CAUCASIAN language (аҧсуа бызшәа), ~190k (Abkhazia) — Northwest Caucasian.** Abkhaz has **one of the world's largest consonant inventories** (~58 — labialized, palatalized, pharyngealized and ejective series) and only **two vowels** (⟨а⟩→[a], ⟨ы⟩→[ə]). A Cyrillic base+modifier scan. **★★ The hallmark is that consonant system**, written with base letters + MODIFIER letters: **⟨ь⟩ palatalizes** (гь→[c], хь→[ç]), **⟨ә⟩ labializes** (гә→[ɡʷ], аҟәа→`aqʼʷa`), **⟨'⟩ pharyngealizes** (х'→[χˤ]); the **three-way voiced/aspirated/ejective** stops & affricates ⟨г қ к⟩→[ɡ kʰ kʼ], ⟨д ҭ т⟩→[d tʰ tʼ], ⟨б ҧ п⟩→[b pʰ pʼ], ⟨ӡ ц ҵ⟩→[d͡z t͡sʰ t͡sʼ], ⟨џ ч ҷ⟩→[d͡ʐ t͡ʃʰ t͡ʃʼ]; the uvular ⟨ҟ⟩→[qʼ], the pharyngeal ⟨ҳ⟩→[ħ], the retroflexes ⟨ж ш⟩→[ʐ ʂ] — plus the extended/historical letters (Ԡ→[lʰ], Ꚁ→[dʷ]…). The system is verified on words: аҷкәын→`at͡ʃʼkʼʷən`, ажәабжь→`aʒʷabʒ`, аҭаацәа→`atʰaat͡ɕʷʰa`. **\*84.0% folded / 93.8% symbol** on wikipron abk_cyrl broad (HUMAN, 205; ~90 letter-definitions + ~115 words), and **55.5% folded / 89.1% symbol** on the 979-word kaikki set. **The folded score is depressed by real hardness, not segment error** (the 89-94% symbol accuracy is the truer signal): the referee's own inconsistency (the letter-*definitions* disagree with the *word*-transcriptions — гә→[kʷ] def vs [ɡʷ] word, хә→[ʍ] def vs [χʷ] word; х~χ, ə~ɨ), the complex **⟨у⟩/⟨и⟩ glide+schwa behaviour** ([w]~[əw], аԥсуа→`apʰsəwa` in the referee), the numeral suffix ⟨-ба⟩→[-pa] (a lexical [b]→[p] devoicing), and loanwords. **🔷 single-source-family** (wikipron + kaikki both Wiktionary; partly *reference-parity* on the letter-definitions). **Outstanding — deferred:** the ⟨у⟩/⟨и⟩ glide+schwa model, the numeral devoicing, stress. |
| **am** Amharic | 89.4% (.83) | kaikki 83.3% | 🟡 | **Ethiopian Semitic in the Ge'ez/Fidäl syllabary — a script model unlike anything else in the fleet:** each codepoint is a whole CV syllable (vowel baked in, no matras/virama), so a flat fidel→CV lookup. Ejectives kʼ tʼ t͡ʃʼ pʼ; the two *unwritten* features are gemination (folded) and the epenthetic 6th-order [ɨ], handled by a phonotactic rule (keep it to break an illegal cluster, drop it where the cluster resyllabifies: አምስት→amɨst). Two independent human referees. **Outstanding:** a small lexical [ɨ] tail the tiny referees disagree on — bounded, no clean rule. |
| **apc** N. Levantine Arabic | 23.2% (.20) | — | 🟡 | **2nd Arabic dialect (North Levantine — Syrian/Lebanese).** Same architecture as Egyptian (`arz`): shares the Arabic engine and applies a one-file shift table over the MSA g2p output; differs from Egyptian in essentially one line — ج→[ʒ] (the Levantine fricative). **Outstanding:** the vowels. Levantine imāla (aː→eː) is pervasive and lexical, and the only dialectal-IPA source is the 410-word referee itself, so a vowel restorer is data-blocked (the same wall as Egyptian). Consonants done. |
| **apd** Sudanese Arabic | single-source (Dickins) | gold (test/arabic.test.ts) | 🔷 | **3rd Arabic dialect (Sudanese, ~54M) — authored, not measured** (no wikipron apd, no free corpus). Authored from published phonology (Dickins) and gold-anchored. Sudanese is the mirror-image of the urban dialects: ق→[ɡ], ج→[ɟ] (voiced palatal — its signature), and it *keeps* the interdentals θ/ð/ðˤ that Egyptian/Levantine merge to stops. **Outstanding:** single-source (🔷) — the distinctive consonants are corroborated against the literature; the shared MSA vowels are the same data tail as arz/apc. |

| **chv** Chuvash | 92.9% (.98) | goldens | 🔷 | **Oghur (Bulgaric) Turkic — Чӑвашла, ~1M (Chuvashia, Russia), Cyrillic; the SOLE surviving Oghur language — the deepest split in Turkic** (sister to every Common-Turkic language the fleet has: tr/az/tk/tt/ba/kk). Vowels ⟨а е и о у ы⟩→[a e i o u ɯ] (⟨е⟩→[je] initial), ⟨в⟩→[ʋ], ⟨х⟩→[χ], ⟨ҫ⟩→[ɕ]; iotation ⟨я ю⟩. **92.9% folded / 97.9% symbol** vs the **English-Wiktionary "Chuvash terms with IPA pronunciation" category (HUMAN, 84 LITERAL `{{IPA|cv|…}}` pairs** — real attestation, *not* module-generated like cdo/bo; pulled via the MediaWiki API — there is no wikipron/epitran chv, and kaikki chv is the same-source 73-pair subset). Folds: the reduced-vowel **breve** (referee [ŏ]/[ø̆]→[ə]/[ɘ] — a preFold, before the backbone strips the breve), the labial approximant w~ʋ (⟨в⟩ coda), **palatalization ʲ** (marked INCONSISTENTLY — выльӑх→[ʋɯlʲəχ] but пӗр→[pɘr]), sibilant place ʂ~ʃ / ʐ~ʑ, uvular~velar x~χ / q~k, the parenthetical optional-segment notation, syllable dots, ⟨а⟩ backness ɑ~a. Residual (6, all 1×) = the referee's **own inconsistencies** — эпӗ→[epɘ] (voiceless, vs the regular эпир→[eˈbir]/эсӗ→[ˈezɘ] which voice), a compound-internal voicing block (ҫулҫӑпӗтӗревҫӗ), and its ə~ɘ swaps (ӑҫта→[ɘɕta]). **🔷 single-source** (one Wiktionary category — thin at 84, but HUMAN and phonologically consistent → the % is a *real* quality signal on a small sample). **Outstanding — deferred:** numbers, a Russian-loan lexicon (like Bashkir's routing), the Viryal (Upper) dialect variant. |
| **ba** Bashkir | 40.8%* (referee-limited) | goldens + Tatar-sibling | 🔷 | **Kipchak Turkic (sibling of Tatar) — Башҡорт теле, ~1.4M (Bashkortostan), Cyrillic.** A Cyrillic grapheme scan. **The HALLMARK is the INTERDENTAL fricatives ⟨ҫ⟩→[θ], ⟨ҙ⟩→[ð]** (Bashkir's signature, shared with Turkmen — аҫыл→[ɑθɯɫ], ҙур→[ðuɾ]). The native g2p is **verified correct on the goldens** (башҡорт→[bɑʂqʊɾt], ҡыҙыл→[qɯðɯɫ], көн→[kʏn]) and ~71% on the non-detected-loan subset. **★ Real Bashkir text is loan-saturated, and Bashkir speakers read loans Russian-style — so the shipped phonemizer DETECTS a Russian loan and routes it to the `ru` g2p.** The detector is a **vowel-harmony violation**: native Bashkir is strictly all-back (а о у ы) or all-front (е и э + ә ө ү), so a word mixing back + front vowels and lacking any Bashkir-specific letter (ҡ ғ ҙ ҫ ң ә ө ү һ — always harmonic) is a loan → routed to Russian (республика→[rʲɪspublʲɪkə], getting the palatalization a Turkic scan can't produce). The eval barely rewards it (the referee's loan forms are a Russian~Bashkir hybrid — Russian palatalization but less vowel reduction — and folding that reduction would corrupt the native ⟨о⟩→[ʊ]), but the shipped behaviour is right for real text. Folds: the vowel-lowering diacritic ̞, reduction breve ̆, rhotic ɾ~r, sibilants ʂ~ʃ/ʐ~ʒ, intervocalic ⟨б⟩ β~b, dark ɫ~l, front-rounded ø~ʏ~ɵ~ʉ~y. **🔷 single-source-family** (kaikki + wikipron bak both Wiktionary; no epitran bak). **Outstanding — deferred:** a Russian-loan LEXICON (the harmonic loans the harmony-heuristic misses, e.g. автобус), numbers, an independent referee. |

The **Lhasa word-tone template** (only syllable 1 is contrastive; non-initial syllables default HIGH + de-aspirate — derived from the data, 95%-supported, Tournadre). **PRIMARY = a hand-curated, INDEPENDENT (non-Wiktionary) referee**: the JIPA "Central Tibetan (Lhasa)" illustration (Zhang 2024, CC-BY), 40 onset/coda/vowel pairs read visually off the article → **100% folded / 100% symbol**. **Independent COVERAGE**: 100% of **299,733** TIBMD@MUC (OpenSLR 124, Minzu Univ., non-Wiktionary) Lhasa syllable tokens phonemize cleanly (numerals aside). **SECONDARY (reported, not floored)** = kaikki Lhasa (1281), which is **MODULE-GENERATED (Module:bo-pron) → 🔷 reference-parity** (NOT independent): 63.9% folded / **89.6% symbol**, deflated by a principled register split we do NOT chase (−d/−g/−s → we emit ʔ, the JIPA *careful* register, vs the module's colloquial vowel-length) + fine 2nd-syllable aspiration. Tone/length/ties folded by the BACKBONE (segmental cf, like hmn/cdo/yue); tone is asserted in test/tibetan.test.ts. **Outstanding — deferred:** the Kham/Amdo reading traditions (numerals, the diminutive འུ diphthong, and vowel-initial glottal onset are now handled). |

The **digraphs** ⟨gb kp⟩→[ɡ͡b k͡p] are the famous **LABIAL-VELARS** (co-articulated single sounds), ⟨dz ts⟩→[d͡z t͡s], ⟨ny⟩→[ɲ]; the **BILABIAL/labiodental contrast** ⟨ƒ⟩→[ɸ] vs ⟨f⟩→[f] and ⟨ʋ⟩→[β] vs ⟨v⟩→[v] (four distinct labial fricatives); ⟨ɖ⟩, ⟨x⟩→[x], ⟨ɣ⟩→[ɰ], ⟨y⟩→[j]. **Nasalization is WRITTEN** (a tilde: ã ẽ ĩ õ ũ ɛ̃ ɔ̃ → kept). **Two allophonies** — and this is the interest — **corroborated by an independent scholarly grammar (Jalloh, *A Phonological and Grammatical Analysis of Ewe*), not just referee-fit:** (1) **⟨w⟩→[w]** before a ROUNDED vowel (o u ɔ) but the unrounded velar approximant **[ɰ]** before an unrounded one ("[ɰ] occurs only before unrounded vowels; /w/ occurs only before rounded vowels"); (2) **⟨r⟩→[l]** in an onset cluster after a consonant (adre→adle, febru→feblu — the /l/~/r/ "variants of each other") but **[r]** elsewhere (loans: Mars→mars). **TONE is not emitted** — it's unmarked in the orthography (the Akan/Shona unwritten-tone situation; a tone lexicon is the deferred path). **100.0% folded / 100.0% symbol** on all **249** kaikki Ewe phonemic pairs (native vocabulary; the near-1:1 orthography + the two allophony rules leave zero segmental residual). Folds: syllable dots, and the **w~ɰ allophone** (we emit Jalloh's rounding-conditioned form for canonical output; the Wiktionary referee generalizes [ɰ], so it's folded to compare). **🔷 single-source-FAMILY** (kaikki + wikipron ewe_latn are both Wiktionary; no epitran ee) — **but the phonology, including the two non-obvious allophony rules, is independently corroborated by Jalloh's grammar**, a meaningful honesty upgrade over pure single-source. **Outstanding — deferred:** TONE (unwritten → needs a tone lexicon), numbers, and the finer dialectal detail Jalloh notes (the alveolar→[r] split, the nasalized-vowel approximant [ɰ̃], palatalization of alveolars before /i/). |

The **narrow referee (48%)** marks sub-phonemic **allophony we don't emit** (intervocalic spirantization b→β̞ d→ð̞ g→ɣ̞, dental d̪ n̪, nasalization ẽ õ) — the low % is that depth gap. NOT 🟢 — the residual info is in the input for the bulk (ezafe = syntax, most homographs = clause context; "a homograph resolvable by sentence context is not 🟢"). NOT ✅ — every available independent referee is convention-confounded (classical register / adversarial / speech-corpus), so no clean *representative* number can be produced (only the convention-neutral backbone certifies), and the residual is real error not the notation/noise ✅ tolerates — though characterized and benign: mostly a single lexically-fixed first vowel (/a/-vs-mid axis), plus the sense/context floor (ezafe + same-POS homographs like کشتی ship/wrestling). The delta, from Walker 1984 + Côté 2012: **affrication** (the signature) /t d/→[t͡s d͡z] before high-front /i y/ + glides /j ɥ/ (tu→t͡sy, dire→d͡ziʁ, dimanche→d͡zimɑ̃ʃ), NOT before back /u/ (tout→tu); **high-vowel laxing** /i y u/→[ɪ ʏ ʊ] in a closed syllable unless the coda is a lengthening /ʁ v z ʒ/ (petite→pt͡sɪt, six→sɪs, jupe→ʒʏp; but dire→d͡ziʁ, musique→myzik stay tense); **word-final /a/→[ɑ]** (Canada→kanadɑ). **No fr-CA corpus exists** (wikipron/kaikki French is France French), so the anchor is a **25-word hand-adjudicated diagnostic gold (100%)**, as for en-GB/en-IN. **Deferred** (fine-grained, variable, no referee): the nasal-vowel shifts (ɛ̃→ẽ, ɔ̃→õ, ɑ̃→ã), long-vowel diphthongisation (père→paɛ̯ʁ, fête→faɪ̯t), and the /ɑ/–/a/ lexical distinction (pâte vs patte, merged by the France engine). |

The front end is **derived, not authored**: kaikki carries both a PFS spelling and a Meixian IPA reading for 6,269 headwords, 4,754 of which align syllable-for-syllable (PFS hyphens × IPA spaces × Han headword length, three lengths that must agree) → `pfs.tsv`, 3,536 keys. The **six-tone system falls out of that pairing** (diacritic × coda → tone, 95–98% pure per row) rather than being assumed. It is a deliberate **dialect translation** — the Sixian SPELLING mapped onto this engine's MEIXIAN phonology, so one language emits one phonology. **89.4% of the wiki's Latin word tokens now read as Hakka**; the 10.6% remainder routes to the foreign reader and is genuinely foreign (Nobel, Castilla, iPhone, quoted English). `Hak-kâ-ngìn` and 客家人 now produce **byte-identical IPA including sandhi** — the two paths are separate artifacts and separate code, which makes that the strongest check this single-source language has. Plus a **normalization layer** (`normalize.ts`, the 6th Sinitic, first built ON `core/sinitic.ts`) and one **derived dict entry** (度, absent from the Meixian source, derived three ways). **Outstanding:** single-source (🔷); the compositional fallback is **81.7% exact held-out** and covers 4.8% of Latin tokens (~0.9% imperfect); cross-word sandhi, sense disambiguation, and the **7.9% single-character Han dict silence** (1,282 characters, 於 ×414 the largest) all deferred. |

Trained on the permissive Nakdimon subset (PD pre-modern + CC-BY-SA wiki). **NUMBERS — a gendered cardinal compositor** (feminine citation; מֵאָה fem vs אֶלֶף/מִילְיוֹן masc multipliers, duals מָאתַיִם/אַלְפַּיִם, decimals). **Cross-validated against THREE independent referees** (Wiktionary lexicographic + Phonikud rule-FST + ReNikud audio-supervised); their **unanimous consensus** (933-word gold, `tools/hebrew/consensus-gold.tsv`) is the objective gate for Phase-1 rule changes — a change ships iff it raises agreement, and where the three disagree it's convention (held, not chased). Two consensus-validated corrections: **proclitic sheva-na → [e]** (word-initial וְ/לְ/בְּ/כְּ/מְ: veʁaʔa; stems stay elided ʃloʃim) and the **glottal rule** (word-initial א/ע → [ʔ], word-final ע → ∅: ʔoʁ, ʔetsba not ʔetsbaʔ). Phase-1 g2p vs the consensus gold **94.1%**. A **consensus lexicon** (`he-lexicon.tsv`, 7800 non-homograph entries) captures Phonikud's modern-vocabulary readings *rendered through our g2p* and kept only where ReNikud independently agrees — a convention-safe lookup layer (known skeleton → our-convention reading; OOV/homographs → the context tagger). Caveats (breadth, not correctness): Phonikud & ReNikud share an author (correlated lineage — the truly-orthogonal fully-independent source is Wiktionary, 2561 vocalized words); the conversational-OOV restorer residual is a characterized, data-blocked cap (the copyright-locked corpus), not an unverified unknown. |

| **hyw** Western Armenian | 86.8% (.83) | narrow (aspiration) | 🔷 | **Indo-European (own Armenian branch), OWN ALPHABET, the Istanbul/diaspora standard (արեւմտահայերէն) — a SIBLING of Eastern hy.** Reuses the shared, manifest-driven Armenian engine (`makeArmenianEngine` — hy/hyw differ only in the consonant table + digraphs; a refactor, not a copy). **★★ THE SIGNATURE is the WESTERN CONSONANT SHIFT** — the classical three-way stop/affricate system collapses to a **two-way** (voiced vs voiceless-aspirated) one: classical **voiced** ⟨բ դ գ ձ ջ⟩ **and** classical **aspirate** ⟨փ թ ք ց չ⟩ both → **[pʰ tʰ kʰ t͡sʰ t͡ʃʰ]** (they *merge*), while classical **voiceless** ⟨պ տ կ ծ ճ⟩ → **voiced [b d ɡ d͡z d͡ʒ]** (պատ→`bɑd`, բարի→`pʰɑɾi`, փակ→`pʰɑɡ`, գործ→`kʰoɾd͡z`, կով→`ɡov`) — mirror-imaging Eastern (պատ→`pɑt`, բարի→`bɑɾi`). Plus **rhotic neutralisation** ⟨ր ռ⟩→[ɾ] (Eastern keeps ɾ vs trill r) and the front-rounded vowel **⟨յու⟩→[ʏ]** *after a consonant* (-ություն→`utʰʏn`) — but the glide [ju] word-initially/post-vowel (յուղ→`juʁ`), and ⟨յո⟩→[jo] (յոթ→`jotʰ` 'seven') — plus the classical **⟨իւ⟩→[ʏ]** spelling. **86.8% folded / 97.8% symbol** on wikipron hye_armn_w_broad (HUMAN, 17211) — it **beats** Eastern's 82.5% on the same referee style — corroborated by hye_armn_w_narrow (17584), which *marks* the aspiration our output emits (բարի→`pʰɑɾí`). One fold: **aspiration ʰ** (the broad primary doesn't transcribe it; the narrow does; the voiced/voiceless-aspirated two-way contrast survives the fold). **★ A trap avoided:** the *narrow* keeps s-clusters voiceless (Ասպետ→ɑsped) but the *broad primary voices them* (Ասպետ→`ɑsbed`) — adding a "s blocks the shift" rule would have broken the primary; the depths genuinely disagree and we follow broad. **🔷 single-source-family** (broad+narrow both wikipron; no epitran hye). **Outstanding — deferred:** numbers verification, the -իա→[jɑ] glide (broad omits it), medial epenthesis. |
| **hy** Armenian | 82.5% (.81) | — | 🔷 | **Indo-European (own branch), OWN ALPHABET, Armenia (~5M) — Eastern Armenian (Yerevan standard).** A cleanroom left-to-right greedy g2p. Signatures: the **three-way stop/affricate system** (⟨բ պ փ⟩=b/p/pʰ, ⟨ձ ծ ց⟩=d͡z/t͡s/t͡sʰ, ⟨ջ ճ չ⟩=d͡ʒ/t͡ʃ/t͡ʃʰ), the **uvulars** ⟨խ⟩→χ / ⟨ղ⟩→ʁ, the **tap ⟨ր⟩→ɾ vs trill ⟨ռ⟩→r**, the ⟨ու⟩=u digraph, and **word-initial glides** ⟨ե⟩→je / ⟨ո⟩→vo / ⟨և⟩→jev (bare e/o/ev elsewhere: Երևան→`jeɾevɑn`). The lever was **Eastern Armenian schwa epenthesis** (Vaux 1998, +7pp): it breaks a word-initial cluster (գն→`ɡən`, խնդ→`χənd`) unless it's an s+stop onset (սպ→`sp`; սկս→`skəs`), and a word-final **rising-sonority** pair (եզր zɾ→`zəɾ`) while a falling coda stays (…ɑɾtʰ). **82.5% folded** on wikipron hye_armn_e broad (HUMAN, 18090), **no folds** (up from 81.1% — the shared `makeArmenianEngine` refactor for Western added a no-vowel citation schwa ⟨Բ⟩→`bə` and a C+glide onset exception ⟨Cյ⟩→`Cj`). The number is **referee-bounded**, not a quality cap: the corpus is very **proper-noun/foreign-name heavy**, and (a) foreign names keep their *source* clusters (Ադրիանա→`ɑdɾjɑn`, un-epenthesised), so blanket **medial** epenthesis is net-negative and deliberately left un-applied, and (b) names carry irregular voicing/aspiration (Աբխազիա→`ɑpʰχ…`). Real-word accuracy ≈ overall (single-letter/acronym noise is only ~1%). Western Armenian (hyw) is now a *sibling engine* sharing this code, not a 2nd referee for Eastern → **🔷 single-source**. **Outstanding:** native-vs-loan discrimination for medial epenthesis (needs a lexicon), and the name voicing/aspiration tail. |
| **hil** Hiligaynon | wikipron 94.4% (.92) | kaikki 94.1% (human) | 🟢 | **Austronesian (Western Bisayan / Ilonggo, ~9M), Latin, near-phonemic — sibling of Cebuano.** A shallow rule g2p mirroring the Cebuano/Tagalog pattern (trigraphs ⟨gui/gue/qui/que⟩ + ⟨ng⟩→ŋ, **word-initial + hiatus glottal** anak→ʔanak/daan→daʔan, **penultimate stress**) with the two **Spanish-loan deltas** read off the referee: **⟨j⟩→[h]** (the Spanish jota, not Cebuano's [d͡ʒ]; Bermejo→beɾmeho) and **⟨f⟩→[p]** (nativised; Fuentes→puentes). **Two independent HUMAN referees** — wikipron hil_latn (proper-noun-heavy, 465) **94.4%** + kaikki hil (native vocab, 477) **94.1%**. Folds: stress (unwritten penult), the unwritten word-final glottal (mango→maŋoʔ, lexical→deferred), the 3-vowel lax allophones (o~ɔ, e~ɛ). **Outstanding:** the Spanish rising diphthongs ⟨ue/ie⟩→[we/je] (origin-ambiguous from spelling — the native hiatus rule gives [uʔe]) + numbers (deferred). |
| **hmn** Hmong (White Hmong) | 94.1% CV (.90) | 100% on-referee | 🔷 | **Hmong-Mien, tonal — White Hmong / Hmoob Dawb (Hmong Daw, `mww`), ~8M (SW China / SE Asia / diaspora).** An **RPA (Romanized Popular Alphabet)→IPA converter** — RPA is the community-standard, deterministic, *phonemic* Latin orthography. Its signature: **there are NO coda consonants, so a word-final ⟨b j v s g m d⟩ is always a TONE marker** (b→˥, v→˧˦, none→˧, j→˥˧, s→˩, m→˩̰ creaky, g→˧˩̤ breathy) — segmentation into onset + rime + tone is unambiguous. The **rich onset system**: prenasalised (np→ᵐb, nts→ᶯd͡ʐ, ntxh→ⁿt͡sʰ), **voiceless sonorants** (hm→m̥, hn→n̥, hl→l̥, hny→ɲ̊), retroflex (r→ʈ, ts→t͡ʂ), uvular (q), palatal (c, ny→ɲ); ⟨tx x⟩ **palatalise** to [t͡ɕ ɕ] before /i/; a vowel-initial syllable takes a glottal [ʔ]; nasal vowels ⟨ee oo⟩→[ẽ ɒ̃]. **The number is stated honestly:** the converter scores **100% on the wikipron mww referee (~455)** — but the maps were *derived from* that referee and RPA is deterministic, so 100% is reference-parity (a consistency check), not independent accuracy. The honest generalisation is the **5-fold held-out CV = 94.1%** (a word-lookup would score ~0 held-out; 94.1% shows the *compositional* onset/rime/tone maps predict unseen RPA syllables). Reliability rests on RPA's determinism (the maps *are* the documented RPA tables) — 61 onsets, of which ~11 (nrh, rh, nph, ml, dl…) are **spec-filled beyond the referee's ~50** since the thin referee doesn't attest them; an onset outside the map falls through as raw RPA (a real edge the held-out CV can't see, as it shares the referee's onset inventory). **🔷 single-source** (wikipron mww, thin ~455; no kaikki/epitran). **Outstanding — deferred:** the Green Hmong / Mong Njua (`hnj`) variety (a servable sibling), tone sandhi, and polysyllabic loan proper-nouns. |

No phonetic referee exists (wikipron/kaikki 404, epitran has no hne mapping). The build is checked against **Kavyopadhyaya's "Grammar of the Chhattisgarhi Dialect" (1921, rev. Grierson)** — its inventory + the attested UDHR sample. That **confirmed श/ष→[s]** (no /ʃ/ — Chhattisgarhi's SOLE confident segmental divergence from Hindi) and **caught a real error**: ऐ/औ are monophthongs [ɛː]/[ɔː] as Hindi (गौरव→[ɡɔrəʋ]), NOT the Bhojpuri diphthongs the first cut blindly copied — corrected. So corrected-Chhattisgarhi = Hindi + श/ष→[s]; its real distinctiveness is grammatical. **Documented-but-unmodelled:** reduced schwa deletion (लोगन→[loɡən]). |

The defining feature: **NO voicing contrast in stops** — the contrast is ASPIRATION (folded), so ⟨b d g⟩ and ⟨p t k⟩ neutralize to [p t k] (bók→pouk, taka→taka, dagur→taɣʏr). Rich **vowel values** ⟨á⟩→[au], ⟨é⟩→[jɛ], ⟨í ý⟩→[i], ⟨ó⟩→[ou], ⟨u⟩→[ʏ], ⟨ú⟩→[u], ⟨æ⟩→[ai], ⟨au⟩→[øy]. **⟨k g⟩→palatal [c]** before a front vowel (gelda→cɛlta), **intervocalic ⟨g⟩→[ɣ]** (or [j] before front: Logi→lɔjɪ). The signature clusters: the **DEVOICED-SONORANT onsets** ⟨hr hl hn⟩→[r l n] / ⟨hj⟩→[ç] / ⟨hv⟩→[kv], the **EPENTHETIC-STOP clusters** ⟨ll⟩→[tl] ⟨rl⟩→[rtl] ⟨rn⟩→[rtn] and ⟨nn⟩→[tn] after a long vowel (fjall→fjatl, Steinn→steitn), **PREASPIRATION** as a full [h] (the fortis geminates ⟨pp tt kk⟩ + a fortis stop before ⟨l m n⟩: Frakki→frahcɪ, vatn→vahtn), and the **pre-velar-nasal change** ⟨ng nk⟩→[ŋk] with the vowel diphthongizing (bang→pauŋk, gengur→ceiŋkʏr). **80.1% folded / 96.8% symbol** vs wikipron isl_latn_broad (10,093 human headwords — LARGE); **98% of the misses are within 2 phones** (only 33 words are 3+), so the segments are essentially right. **FOLDED:** vowel LENGTH (syllable-quantity) and **ASPIRATION** (the phonemic-but-positional pre/post-aspiration — the main deferred subsystem), plus devoiced sonorants (the ring auto-strips) and short-vowel quality (ɛ→e, ɔ→o, ʏ→y). **Measured:** the ⟨rl⟩→[rtl] epenthesis beats the alternatives (79.6 vs 78.8/78.1). Residual = the loan ⟨f⟩~[v] + vowel inconsistency (Safír), and the ⟨ei ey⟩-palatalization minority (Geir→keir). **🔷 single-source but LARGE.** **Outstanding — deferred:** the aspiration (pre/post) model, vowel length, numbers. |

| **ka** Georgian | 99.8% folded (.99) | wikipron (20894, human) | 🔷 | **Kartvelian (its OWN family — a fleet first), Georgia (~4M), the Mkhedruli script.** Georgian orthography is essentially **one-letter-one-phoneme** (a transparent 33-letter alphabet, no digraphs), so the g2p is a greedy table scan + **one context rule**. The 33-letter table was **derived empirically from 18,350 1:1-aligned wikipron words**, confirming (and correcting two guesses): the three-way **VOICED / ASPIRATED / EJECTIVE** stop-and-affricate contrast (ბ b · ფ pʰ · პ pʼ; დ d · თ tʰ · ტ tʼ; …), the **uvulars ღ=ʁ** (voiced) / **ხ=χ** (voiceless — NOT velar ɣ/x) / **ყ=qʼ** (ejective), and 5 vowels a ɛ i ɔ u. The one rule: **word-final voiced stops devoice to aspirated** (⟨დ⟩→tʰ, ⟨ბ⟩→pʰ, ⟨გ⟩→kʰ — categorical in the referee, 1584/1585 for ⟨დ⟩; the -ად adverbial: კარგად→kʼaɾɡatʰ). **Scored 99.8% folded / 100% symbol** on the **wikipron kat_geor_narrow referee (HUMAN, 20,894 words)** — near the top of the fleet; the 49 residuals are Asomtavruli single-letter entries + letter-citation forms + a loanword edge (asphalt ⟨ფ⟩→f). The BACKBONE strips the referee's combining diacritics (ä→a, e̞→e, o̞→o) + ties; the folds handle the rest of the narrow allophony (mid-vowel symbol e→ɛ/o→ɔ, dark ɫ→l, pre-velar n→ŋ, the ვ labialisation ʷ→v, ⟨ყ⟩'s [χʼ]→qʼ). **🔷 single numeric source** (wikipron only — no kaikki dump / epitran kat committed) but the referee is LARGE + human and the orthography transparent, so each letter rests on hundreds–thousands of aligned tokens → the inventory is strongly attested. Stress (weak/non-contrastive) + numbers deferred. |
| **kam** Kamba | 100% folded (5 words) | Wiktionary (5) + Omniglot/Wikipedia | 🔷 | **Niger-Congo BANTU (E55) — Kikamba, Kenya (~4M), Latin orthography.** A PURE greedy longest-match g2p (the Kikuyu pattern — no code rules, the Bantu fricativization/prenasalization live in the grapheme table). Kamba shares Kikuyu's **7-vowel ATR where the TILDE is vowel QUALITY not nasalization** (⟨ĩ⟩=e, ⟨ũ⟩=o — CONFIRMED by the anchor mũtĩ→mòté), length-by-doubling, ⟨ny⟩=ɲ, ⟨ng'⟩=ŋ, and prenasalized ⟨mb nd ng⟩. But the **consonants DIVERGE**: **⟨v⟩=β** (Kamba spells the Bantu [β] as ⟨v⟩, not ⟨b⟩ — the biggest difference; standalone ⟨b⟩ also →β for the mission/Bible spelling), a **⟨sy⟩=ʃ / ⟨ky⟩=tʃ** palatal series Kikuyu lacks, **NO ⟨c⟩ / NO ⟨g⟩=ɣ** ([ɡ] only post-nasally), and **⟨nth⟩=ⁿð**. TONE (2-tone H/L) is not written → not emitted; the referee's tone marks + syllable dots + prenasal notation + length are folded. **Scored 5/5 folded (100%) / symbol 100%** — but the referee is **THIN: en.wiktionary Kamba has only 5 IPA words** (no kaikki dump / epitran kam-Latn / wikipron), and those 5 exercise only the **vowels + prenasals** (ĩ=e/ũ=o, ᵐb/ᵑɡ, ɲ) — **NOT** the divergent consonants (⟨v⟩=β, ⟨sy⟩=ʃ, ⟨ky⟩=tʃ, ⟨th⟩=ð), which rest on two curated authorities (the Omniglot Kikamba alphabet chart + the Wikipedia Kamba consonant table, cites Roberts-Kohno 2000), not a second numeric referee. **Open/contested (documented):** ⟨th⟩ voicing [ð]~[θ] (went [ð] per Omniglot/Wikipedia + Kikuyu parallel); ⟨sy⟩/⟨ky⟩ single-sourced. **🔷 single thin source** — a wider human referee would upgrade it. Tone + numbers deferred. |
| **kea** Kabuverdianu | 7/7 anchors (100%) | kaikki (7 IPA words) | 🔷 | **Portuguese-lexified CREOLE of Cape Verde (kriolu, ~870k) — the FIRST creole with a bespoke engine in the fleet; the Santiago (Sotavento/Badiu) variety.** Written in the **ALUPEC/AK** unified orthography (a standardized PHONEMIC alphabet), so the engine is a greedy ALUPEC scan + Portuguese-creole nasalization + **stress** (a written accent; else the Ibero default — **penult** if the word ends in a vowel/-s, **oxytone** if it ends in a consonant: mudjer→muˈd͡ʒeɾ) + falling-diphthong offglides (oitu→ˈoitu). Signatures: the DIGRAPHS ⟨dj⟩→d͡ʒ / ⟨tx⟩→t͡ʃ / ⟨nh⟩→ɲ / ⟨lh⟩→ʎ / ⟨rr⟩→r, ⟨x⟩→ʃ, ⟨j⟩→ʒ, single ⟨r⟩→ɾ tap, ⟨s⟩→[s] always (ALUPEC writes ⟨z⟩ for [z]); the acute marks the OPEN mid quality (é→ɛ, ó→ɔ) + stress; ⟨a⟩→[ɐ]; **nasalization** — a coda ⟨n/m⟩ nasalizes the vowel and surfaces as [ŋ] before a velar (tabanka→tɐˈbãŋkɐ), else absorbed (sénpri→sɛ̃pɾi, bon→bõ). **AUTHORED — no machine referee exists** (wikipron has no kea; the kaikki dump carries IPA on only **7 words**), so validation is the falsifiable **gold test** (test/kabuverdianu.test.ts): the engine reproduces all **7 kaikki IPA anchors** at the segment level (the kaikki's onset-ˈ notation + its loose ⟨r⟩~ɾ are notation diffs, not errors). **🔷 thin single-source** — one of the fleet's thinnest anchors (7 words vs Luo 17 / Madurese 35), but ALUPEC is one-letter-≈-one-sound so the g2p is largely deterministic. **Outstanding — deferred:** the Barlavento (São Vicente) variety, numbers, a larger referee (none exists). |
| **ki** Kikuyu | 99.4% folded (.98) | Wiktionary (1062) | 🔷 | **Niger-Congo BANTU (E51) — the largest language of Kenya (~8M), Latin orthography.** A PURE greedy longest-match g2p (no code rules — the fricativization + prenasalization live entirely in the grapheme table), **99.4% folded** on a LARGE 1062-word en.wiktionary Kikuyu referee — the highest folded score in the fleet, and the 6 residuals are referee glide-edge/vowel-quality variants → backbone ~99.5%. Signatures: a **7-vowel ATR system where the TILDE is vowel QUALITY not nasalization** (⟨ĩ⟩=e, ⟨ũ⟩=o, ⟨e⟩=ɛ, ⟨o⟩=ɔ — Gĩkũyũ→ɣekojo); Bantu **FRICATIVIZATION** ⟨b⟩=β / ⟨th⟩=ð / ⟨g⟩=ɣ / ⟨c⟩=ɕ; **PRENASALIZED** digraphs ⟨mb⟩=ᵐb / ⟨nd⟩=ⁿd / ⟨nj⟩=ᶮdʑ / ⟨ng⟩=ᵑɡ; ⟨ng'⟩=ŋ (distinct from ⟨ng⟩), ⟨ny⟩=ɲ, ⟨r⟩=ɾ. **Dahl's Law is orthographic** (a dissimilated k is spelled ⟨g⟩→ɣ) → no rule. **TONE** (2-tone H/L + downstep) is not written (contextual + grammatical) → not emitted; the referee's tone/downstep/length/glide notation is folded. Referee built by `tools/corpus/build-referee.ts` (batched); phonology corroborated by TWO authorities — the Englebretson Gĩkũyũ sketch grammar + Wahome & Subiyanto (2023), whose vowel/consonant phoneme tables independently confirm the mapping (esp. the tilde-is-quality ⟨ĩ⟩=e/⟨ũ⟩=o). **Outstanding — deferred:** tone, numbers. **🔷 single-source** (Wiktionary only, but LARGE + human). |
| **crh** Crimean Tatar | 94.4%* (.99) | — | 🔷 | **Kipchak Turkic with strong Oghuz influence (qırımtatar tili), ~540k (Crimea + diaspora), the Turkish-based LATIN alphabet** (highly phonemic, no digraphs — ⟨ç ş ñ ğ⟩ are single letters). A greedy grapheme scan + gemination + word-final stress. **The orthography WRITES the uvular/velar choice**: uvular ⟨q⟩→[q] / the voiced dorsal ⟨ğ⟩→[ɣ] vs velar ⟨k g⟩; the **front/back harmony vowels** ⟨a o u ı⟩→[ɑ o u ɯ] vs ⟨e ö ü i⟩→[e ø y i]; ⟨c⟩→[d͡ʒ], ⟨ç⟩→[t͡ʃ], ⟨ş⟩→[ʃ], ⟨ñ⟩→[ŋ], ⟨j⟩→[ʒ]; ⟨â⟩→[a]; a **doubled letter geminates** → [Cː]/[Vː] (yollamaq→`jolːɑmɑq`, şeer→`ʃeːr`). ★ **Turkish-I casing** handled (dotless capital ⟨I⟩→[ɯ]→ı, dotted ⟨İ⟩→[i]; both in the token class — Qırım→`qɯˈrɯm`). **\*94.4% folded / 98.9% symbol (17/18) — but on a TINY 18-word referee** (English Wiktionary "Crimean Tatar terms with IPA pronunciation", the ONLY source — no wikipron/kaikki/epitran crh), so the number confirms the vowel + uvular mappings rather than proving broad accuracy; the g2p rests on the phonemic Latin orthography + goldens. The one residual is cañı→`d͡ʒanɯ`, where the referee wrote ⟨ñ⟩ as [n] (standard ⟨ñ⟩=[ŋ] → **ours** is correct). **🔷 thin single-source.** **Outstanding — deferred:** numbers, a larger/independent referee, a Cyrillic front-end, the ⟨â⟩ palatalisation of the preceding consonant. |

| **kl** Kalaallisut (Greenlandic) | 94.8% (.90) · 98.5% symbol | wikipron broad (human, 1581) | 🔷 | **Eskimo-Aleut (Inuit branch), West Greenlandic, ~56k (Greenland), the 1973 PHONEMIC Latin orthography — THE FLEET'S FIRST ESKIMO-ALEUT language** (a new family). A near-1:1 scan (the orthography is highly phonemic — Finnish/Māori-tier). **★ THREE-VOWEL system /a i u/** (the Inuit hallmark): a→[a], i→[i], u→[u]; **⟨e o⟩ are NOT phonemes** — they are the LOWERED allophones of /i u/ the orthography writes before a uvular ⟨q r⟩, so **⟨e⟩→[i], ⟨o⟩→[u]** (aaneq→aːniq, nanoq→nanuq). **★ the UVULAR stop ⟨q⟩→[q]** + the uvular fricative **⟨r⟩→[ʁ]**; **⟨ng⟩→[ŋ], ⟨nng⟩→[ŋː]**; a **doubled letter → LENGTH** (illu→ilːu, aallaat→aːlːaːt); loan **⟨b d⟩→[p t]** (Greenlandic has no voiced stops: Bolatta→pulatːa); ⟨ts⟩→[ts]. No stress (weight-based, unmarked in the referee). **94.8% folded / 98.5% symbol** vs wikipron kal_latn_broad (HUMAN, 1581) — **a clean, high-scoring bring-up** (a transparent orthography + a SINGLE-dialect referee = a tight measurement, unlike the multi-dialect Celtic ones; the raw-exact 19% is a length-notation artifact fully handled by the folds). Folds: the length notation (the referee writes a long vowel/consonant as a DOUBLED segment; we emit [Vː]/[Cː] but the eval backbone strips ː → collapse the referee's doubling to a single — the Faroese lesson), and ʁ~r (the coarse broad-referee rhotic). **The ~6% residual is entirely the DEFERRED NARROW phonetic layer** that leaked into a few "broad" entries — the uvular vowel-lowering (o→[ɔ], e→[ɜ]), the regressive consonant **ASSIMILATION** (the famous Inuit sandhi: anarnaq→[anɑnnɑq]), and ⟨ll⟩→[ɬ] (alleroq→[aɬɬɜʁɔq]). **🔷 single-source family** (wikipron; kaikki Greenlandic same Wiktionary source) but a transparent orthography → low referee-error risk. **Outstanding — deferred:** the phonetic assimilation + uvular-lowering NARROW layer (a lexical/phonetic refinement), numbers. |
| **km** Khmer | 65.8% (.63) rules · **77.7% freq-wt** · **94.0% shipped real-text vs independent** | google-lexicon (62,093; non-Wiktionary) + aakanee (NC, out-of-band) | ✅ | **Austroasiatic (Mon-Khmer), the Khmer SESQUISYLLABIC abugida — one of the hardest scripts in the fleet; NON-tonal.** Khmer's defining feature is the **two consonant series**: every base consonant is a-series (inherent ɑː) or o-series (inherent ɔː), and the SAME vowel sign is read differently by series — ក+ា = **kaː** but គ+ា = **kiə** (same sign ⟨ា⟩). A proper **unit-based syllabifier per Huffman (1970)**: **governance** (a vowel's series is set by the LAST PRECEDING dominant stop/spirant, tracked across the word so passive-initial syllables harmonise — ផ្ទះ→pʰteəh, ចេតនា→ceːtnaː); **inherent-vowel length by coda** (presyllable short ɑ/ɔ, stressed-open long ɑː/ɔː, stressed-closed LONG on a plain coda កង→kɑːŋ/គង→kɔːŋ but SHORT on a silent-subscript/doubled or bantaq coda ចន្ទ→cɑn/រដ្ឋ→ruət/កាត់→kat); **coda assignment** with the nasal medial-cluster split (កណ្ដាល→kɑnɗaːl, តម្រង→tɑmrɑːŋ) and medial-bare-as-coda (គីមឈី→kiːmcʰiː); **multi-char vowels** ⟨ះ⟩/⟨ំ⟩ (កោះ→kɑh, ជុំ→cum); ⟨ប៉⟩→p; ិ o-series→ɨ. **RULES 65.8% folded / 53.3% raw / 87.0% symbol / 77.7% frequency-weighted** (independent — `phonemizeWordRules` reads neither lexicon, which is what keeps the referee non-circular). Trajectory 35.6 → 38.6 → 55.0 → 56.0 → 60.0 → 65.8%. Folds normalise real referee noise (optional minor-syllable ʔ per Huffman IX.B.2, breve short-vowel notation, INCONSISTENT aspiration kʰ~k both ways — our output keeps canonical aspiration). **INDEPENDENT VOWELS — a whole letter class the syllabifier deleted:** all 17 of U+17A3–U+17B3 fell through a Phase-1 `continue` and were dropped from the reading (176,282 corpus occurrences, 6.0% of all Khmer-letter tokens); the standalone case only *looked* healthy because the lexicon covers 8 of them and production is lexicon-first. Sourced from three independent sources, which was necessary — ឬ has ZERO word-initial dictionary entries because it is itself a word. On the 254 referee words containing one, agreement went **0/254 → 70/254** — not poor accuracy on a hard class, every single one wrong. **SEGMENTATION — Khmer writes no inter-word space**, so a maximal run is several words and the syllabifier re-parses across boundaries it cannot see: measured on 4,000 junctions where a writer actually typed U+200B, joining two words CORRUPTS the reading **54.6%** of the time (នៅ|សតវត្ស → *nɨwhtɑʋɑt* joined against *nɨw* + *sɑtɑʋoət* apart, a coda stolen and a syllable lost). Supervision came from where Cambodians put the breaks — 2.85M writer-typed ZWSP boundaries — and the hard part was deciding WHICH ZEROS TO BELIEVE (a typed ZWSP is a positive a human placed; its absence may mean "no boundary" *or* "the writer did not bother"), solved with a density-calibrated per-split-point rate and an explicit ABSTAIN band for compounds the corpus does not answer. Three tiers, all scored on one seed-0 held-out split: unigram Viterbi 66.8% F1 (`segment.ts`, retained for the ៗ rule, which needs only the final boundary); an **averaged perceptron 85.5% F1 — the SYNC default, on**, dependency-free (a 42,643-weight Map, not an ONNX graph), 79.2% corpus end-to-end and **80.4% against independent human gold**; and a **BiLSTM 89.0% F1 as the ASYNC tier** (83.5% / 81.3%), which needs the optional `onnxruntime-node`, so `phonemize` can never benefit from it — the same shape as Hebrew's nakdan. With NO segmentation those end-to-end figures are 44.7% / 36.4%. ⚠ The jump from 66.8 to 85.5 is almost entirely the LABEL CLEANING, not the model. ⚠ The frequency segmenter CANNOT do this job for a structural reason rather than a tuning one — it recovers 0 of 12 month compounds because ខែ, មករា *and* ខែមករា are all in its vocabulary and splitting always pays an extra −log(p). ⚠ The perceptron costs ~2.6× the sync path's time (2.4 → 6.2 ms per 3,480 chars), recorded because it is a real hot-path regression and accepted because it fixes more than half of all word junctions. **THREE LEXICON TIERS, because the rule-unpredictable residual is genuinely LEXICAL** (inherent-vowel length ចន្ទ *can* vs កង *kɑːŋ*, internal-doubling minor-syllable/coda ambiguity, Pali loanword vowels) — the Romanian-stress / akan-tone pattern. The shipped `phonemizeWord` is dict-first, and the order is an EVIDENCE RANKING: a **2,822-word wikipron-verified exceptions lexicon** (`km-lexicon.tsv`, CC-BY-SA), then a **557-entry kaikki tier** (`km-lexicon-kaikki.tsv`, CC-BY-SA — en.wiktionary readings for words the wikipron SCRAPE lacks; same lineage as the referee, which is why it can be a lexicon and never a referee; its conversion validates **97.7%** against wikipron on 6,564 shared words, derived after de-fusing a wiktextract artifact that welds the initial letter's NAME onto the romanization), then a **56,356-entry tier converted from google/language-resources' Khmer pronunciation dictionary** (CC BY 4.0 — an INDEPENDENT source, though a lexicon rather than a referee). The kaikki tier overrides Google on 196 shared words and gives **361 words their first evidence of any kind**; against the independent aakanee source its rows score **83.8%, the best tier accuracy** (Google's 78.0%), and shipped-vs-aakanee moved 79.5 → **81.9%** (80.7 before the vowel sweep). Gates as always: no referee word, rules must disagree (536 skipped where the rules already match — the rules are right on half of new Wiktionary vocabulary), malformed rows rejected. Running-text lexicon coverage 14.7% → 23.4%. ⚠ **The second tier is UNMEASURABLE ON AVAILABLE GOLD BY CONSTRUCTION**, stated rather than dressed up: `km-lexicon.tsv` is an EXCEPTIONS lexicon (mined where the rules DISAGREE with wikipron), so for any referee word absent from it the rules ALREADY match by construction — a naive merge REGRESSED exactly those 3,486 words, 100% → 88.1% — so they are excluded, which removes every scorable word. The +15pp (rules 63.3% vs converted dictionary 78.3% on the 5,734 comparable referee words) is a TRANSFER argument, not a measurement of what ships. ⚠ An earlier estimate of this tier's worth was **7× too high**, and the error was conceptual: raw dictionary coverage (60.4%) compared against *exceptions*-lexicon coverage (14.7%); decomposed by the evidence available per token, the reachable population is 8.7%. Invariants pinned mechanically: no dictionary entry may shadow a wikipron entry, and no referee word may leak into the dictionary tier. **NORMALIZATION (done):** the tokenizer is a deliberately minimal three-way split, so every symbol it does not name was skipped in SILENCE — corpus DROP 165 → 28. The iteration mark ៗ ("repeat the preceding WORD", 24,413 occurrences, the language's single largest defect) is U+17D7 and falls in the gap between ៓ and ៜ, so it matched no group at all — which is what forced the segmenter, since the rule needs to know where a word ends. Grouped numbers de-group across a SPACE or ZWSP as well as a comma (567 corpus instances), and the comma is ALSO a decimal separator — de-grouping runs first and consumes every comma introducing a three-digit block, so whatever survives is a decimal; that ordering is the whole discrimination. Native digits are **74%** of this corpus, so every pattern matches both ranges — `\d` alone would have missed 68% of digit-runs. Migrated onto the shared symbol tier: four currencies (៛ € £ plus a `US$` multi-character key, since the bare `$` cannot reach past the Latin-letter guard), the exponent ² as a **suffix** (ម៉ែត្រការេ — `compound` puts the measure word first, reversing the corpus's own order), ×, ÷, ±, and បូក for the spaced plus (312 sites, ZERO carrying a LaTeX or C marker within ±40 chars — spacing is the discriminator, and the spaced instances are Khmer grammar formulas and algebra). ⚠ Two core fixes the migration forced: **`\s` DOES NOT MATCH U+200B**, Khmer's word separator and its single most frequent cell (33,285) — migrating naively LOST the ZWSP handling the local rules had and made the artifact scan *worse*; and a unit key must be lowercase, or declaring ⟨°C⟩ is not a missed match but a TypeError the first time that unit appears in real text. **Refusals recorded with their measurements** rather than quieted: the DECIMAL POINT is unsourceable (ចុច ×157 and ក្បៀស ×18 both fail on SENSE — neither occurs between digits once, and ចុច's collocations make it the verb "to press"), `=` stays digit-flanked (1,158 spaced Khmer=Khmer definitional glosses and 694 code-shaped against 66 arithmetic — spacing does NOT separate them, because the glosses are spaced too), លើសូន្យ is refused as an unattested composition (the zu/xh `Kristu` mistake), and `CN¥` is a documented gap (the yuan word is unattested — យ័ន ×436 looks frequent until every hit is read as a substring of បាយ័ន/អារ្យ័ន, the spaceless-script inflation `corpus-words.ts` warns about). **THREE PASS-2 CONSUMPTION GUARDS + A NASAL MERGE (+3.8pp folded, 268 words gained, ZERO lost).** Three rules in coda assignment DELETE a unit after taking its `ons[0]` as a coda, and none asked whether that unit was already carrying a coda of its own — so a whole syllable vanished. កញ្ចក់ read *kɑɲ* against kɑɲcɑʔ (the trailing rule sliced away a unit the bantaq rule had just given a coda); កំណត់ read *kɑmn*; and ចំណង read the garbled *cɑmnŋɑː* because the medial rule stole the ONSET of the final syllable, stranding ⟨ង⟩ as its own syllable with a long inherent vowel. Separately, ⟨ំ⟩ carries its own [-m] and was rendering it alongside a ⟨ង⟩ coda — កម្លាំង *kɑmlamŋ* against kɑmlaŋ. ⚠ **The merge condition is the ASSIGNED CODA, not the spelling**: bucketing the referee by the consonant after ⟨ំ⟩ shows ⟨ង⟩ is the only one that drops the m (68 of 88; ណ 102/102, ព 64/64, កាំបិត is *k a m ɓ ə t*), but reading the exceptions individually finds ជំងឺ *cum.ŋɨː* and ទំងន់ *tum.ŋuən* keeping it — in both, ⟨ង⟩ is an ONSET. A spelling rule breaks both; PASS 2 has already decided the question. Per-class: the medial-bare shape went **0.0% → 93.3%** (every one of those 30 words was wrong — the same total-failure signature as the independent vowels), the bantaq shape 55.0% → 71.4%, ⟨ំ⟩+⟨ង⟩ 3.4% → 63.6%. ⚠ **440 exceptions-lexicon rows are now redundant** — entries the lexicon was memorising that the rules now derive. Not pruned (the lexicon wins at lookup, so shipped output is unchanged, and pruning would alter 440 raw readings for no measurable gain), but recorded as evidence the fix GENERALISED rather than special-cased. **AN END-TO-END MEASUREMENT AT LAST — 74.8% on held-out vocabulary.** Every previously published km number was the RULES number; what ships is dict-first and had never been measured. ⚠ **The obvious experiment answers nothing**, and the construction says why: `referee ∩ exceptions = 2,822 of 2,822` (mined FROM the referee) while `referee ∩ dictionary = 0 of 56,355` (the builder excludes every referee word by design) — so masking the exceptions lexicon and scoring the referee just reproduces the rules number, because the dictionary tier cannot contribute a single word. What works is rebuilding BOTH tiers against an 80% split, with the dictionary's **exclusion gate seeing only the 80%** so held-out words legitimately enter it. On the 1,296 held-out words: rules **69.0%**, shipped dict-first **74.8%** (62.3/73.1 before the vowel sweep — the rules closed most of the tier gap themselves). The dictionary answers 87% of unseen vocabulary at **78.8%** accuracy — closely reproducing the 78.3% the earlier comparable-population argument predicted, which makes it an out-of-sample confirmation rather than a restatement. ⚠ Word path only; running text also depends on boundary restoration (its own 80.4% against human gold). **A SECOND REFEREE — AND A THIRD SOURCE.** The "no second independent referee" caveat was conflating two circularities: the google/language-resources dictionary cannot referee the SHIPPED path (its dict tier IS that data), but the eval scores `phonemizeWordRules`, which reads no lexicon — so it was a legitimate second referee all along, and it is CC BY 4.0, committable. Wired as the SECONDARY (the af/RCRL move): `km.google-lexicon.tsv`, 62,093 rows, ALL Khmer words including the wikipron-settled ones the lexicon tier excludes. **49.4% folded / 86.1% symbol / 75.5% frequency-weighted — the symbol accuracy agrees with the primary's 87.0% to 0.9pp across two independent traditions and a 9x vocabulary difference** (after two justified per-referee notation folds: ʋ~w, where the referees write the SAME phoneme with opposite letters 120:1 vs 1133:288, and ou~oː, a total ូ-convention split 471:6 vs 0:9151; ə~ɨ was checked identically and left UNFOLDED — both referees use both at ~2.4:1, lexical variance a fold would hide). The lower word-exact is the expected shape (a TTS dictionary skews to long compounds, and the two sources themselves agree only ~78% word-exact where they overlap, which bounds any engine score). ⚠ The shipped path must NEVER be scored against this referee — the non-circularity lives entirely in scoring the rules. A THIRD lineage exists and corroborates out-of-band: the aakanee.com dictionary (via open-dict-data/ipa-dict, 3,276 words) — **CC BY-NC-SA, so measurement-only, never committed** (the Urdu NC arrangement; `tools/khmer/aakanee-crosscheck.ts`, data outside the tree). It triangulates everything at once: aakanee-vs-wikipron **93.1%** on 1,696 overlapping words (source health — clears Urdu's 88.2% bar), rules-vs-aakanee **74.4%** (68.2 before the sweep — the +6.2pp move in step with the primary is the overfit check), shipped-vs-aakanee **81.9%**, with the shipped−rules delta independently reproducing the held-out gap, and the dictionary-tier rows at 77.3% = Google-vs-aakanee cross-validation landing on the 78.3%/78.8% both earlier estimates predicted. Three routes, one answer. **THE RESIDUAL, WORKED AS DERIVED CLASSES (+9.8pp in one sweep: 56.0 → 65.8%, day A/B +624/−31).** The 2,365-word residual was clustered by edit type and every rule-shaped class was DERIVED from the referee and fixed: ⟨ៈ⟩ yuukaleapintu was sitting in the diacritics list and silently skipped (aʔ, 69:8); the bantaq re-colours o-series ⟨ា⟩ to oə (71:1 — while the silent-subscript shape keeps iə 11:0, a split that falls out structurally); o-series ⟨េ⟩ is ei (186:86); non-final ⟨ិ⟩/⟨ី⟩ reduce (i/e vs the stressed table values, 110:36); a CLOSED o-series presyllable is u (ង/ល codas uə); ⟨ី⟩+coda is iː in any position (135:6); ⟨ដ្ឋ⟩ reads tt (59:38); ⟨័រ⟩ samyok+silent-រ is oə (25:1). Two trial classes MEASURED AND REVERTED (open-presyllable eə, medial ⟨ប⟩→p — both regressed; the derivations were contaminated). ⚠ A substring bug in one derivation ("i" matches inside "iː") shortened every ⟨ី⟩ word's RAW output while leaving the folded metric unchanged — four goldens caught it; the folded gain was real, the raw fix is iː. ⚠ **NOT REFEREE-OVERFIT**: rules-vs-aakanee (independent) moved 68.2 → 74.4% (+6.2pp) in step with the primary's +5.8pp. What remains is genuinely LEXICAL — medial-syllable structural ambiguity (កាកបាទ kak-ɓaʔ vs kak-a-ɓaʔ, no spelling cue), the Pali register's vowels (ɔ~eə on initial presyllables, medial ɓ~p), ə~ɨ — the class Huffman himself calls unpredictable, and the three lexicon tiers carry it. **THE REAL-TEXT NUMBERS (freq/km.txt — this repo's own 2.85M writer-boundary harvest):** rules alone **77.7% token-weighted** (vs 65.8% word-exact: the referee is dictionary-shaped and over-samples rare Pali compounds, the af pattern); the SHIPPED path **99.7%** against the wikipron-verified readings and **94.0% against the fully-independent aakanee — AT the 93.1% ceiling where the two human sources agree with each other**. On real text the shipped engine is as close to aakanee as wikipron is. **Verdict ✅ (the full arc: 🔵🔷 → 🔷 → 🟡 → ✅):** trust the output. The case, point by point against the legend: the word-exact referee % is deflated by dictionary shape (77.7% token-weighted vs 65.8%) and bounded by inter-source disagreement (the independent human sources agree only 78–93% with each other; the shipped path scores 94.0% against one of them on real text — the ceiling); the residual decomposes into a characterized lexical tail carried by three lexicon tiers (wikipron-verified → kaikki → Google, 59k+ entries, invariants pinned) plus convention variance between transcription traditions; and the evidence is MULTI-SOURCE — two committed referees of different lineage, a third independent source corroborating out-of-band, and every engine-rule class derived from data with its counts recorded. Caveats stated, not hidden: held-out word-exact on dictionary-shaped vocabulary is 74.8% (rare Pali compounds miss); segmentation is its own subsystem with its own honest figure (80.4% vs human gold, sync tier); tone does not exist (non-tonal) and stress is not modelled. **Deferred, sized, declined:** a BiLSTM OOV tier (~+3pp type-level on the 7.4% no-evidence tail, the ur/dakshina sizing); the ə~ɨ lexical tail; the async BiLSTM segmenter's +4.3pp junction gap (optional-dependency-locked). |
| **kmr** Kurmanji | 97.4% (.95) | epitran 96.3% | ✅ | **Northern Kurdish (Iranian) — the LATIN (Hawar) alphabet.** The key contrast with its Iranian siblings: Persian/Pashto use the Perso-Arabic *abjad* and need short-vowel restoration (the unwritten-vowel wall), but **Kurmanji writes its vowels**, so it's a near-phonemic Latin g2p — no restoration, much more reliable. Signature: **⟨c⟩→d͡ʒ / ⟨ç⟩→t͡ʃ** (reverse of Romance), ⟨j⟩→ʒ, ⟨ş⟩→ʃ, ⟨q⟩→q, ⟨x⟩→x, ⟨xw⟩→xʷ; long a/ê/î/o/û vs short e/i/u; n→ŋ before k/ɡ; final stress. Two independent referees both ~96%+. **With text normalization** (kmr.wikipedia dump, 182,432 paragraphs). **The defining rule is the BOUND SUFFIX ON A NUMERAL — 287 instances in 451 mined segments**, the densest thing in the corpus: Kurmanji writes its oblique/ezafe and its ordinal after the DIGITS (`sala 2015an`, `roja 25ê`, `salên 1990î`, `sedsala 5em`, `Çapa 1emîn`) and each was read as its own word with its own primary stress. Trap 14's fix shape — words first, suffix attached inside the rule — **and the written glide has to be RE-DERIVED, not copied**, because the writer picked it from the digits while it belongs to the spoken form (`2ê` is *duyê*; `1980yî` is *heştêyî*). Both separator conventions are present and are told apart by GROUP SIZE (period+3 ×75 thousands, comma+1 ×55 decimal, and period+2 ×6 is NEITHER — versions, a date, a clock). `ji sedî` is the percent word — the bare `sedî` ×113 is the poet **Sa'di** — and `çargoşe`/`kûp` are the powers, both GLOSSED AGAINST THE SYMBOL by the wiki's own unit articles, where `kare` ×41 is the verb "can". The minus is claimed inside the DEGREE rule, the only place it is unambiguous (10/10 genuine negatives are temperatures). DROP 92→24, 201/450 utterances changed. **Outstanding:** the unwritten allophony the Hawar spelling can't determine — **aspiration** (kʰ/pʰ/tʰ) and **pharyngealisation** (folded), and the r/rr tap-trill Hawar collapses; the DECIMAL SEPARATOR is removed without a word because **no Kurmanji reading for it is attested anywhere this repo can reach** (`vîrgul` ×1 is a magazine, `xal` is the geometric point and the maternal uncle, `dehik`/`xala dehiyî` ×0) — a definitive negative, and `sources.ts` prescribes exactly this fallback. |
