Dravidian short/long e·o, dental vs retroflex, geminate→length. Two independent human referees corroborate — **among the cleanest in the fleet.** **Outstanding:** none of substance — diffuse notation (visarga, o~ɔ). |
| **ko** Korean | 58.5% (.52) | — | ✅ | **Referee-limited** (narrow-transcription allophony: ㄹ ɭ~ɾ, intervocalic voicing) — the % is not a quality signal. |
| **ky** Kyrgyz | 90.7% (.89) | — | 🔷 | **Turkic (Kipchak), Kyrgyzstan (~5M), Cyrillic — sibling of Kazakh.** A cleanroom left-to-right g2p (the `kk` Cyrillic-scan shape); vowel harmony is *spelled*, so no harmony needs predicting. **The lever is the velar/uvular harmony that Kyrgyz does NOT spell** (unlike Kazakh's separate ⟨қ⟩/⟨ғ⟩ letters): ⟨к⟩→[q] / ⟨г⟩→[ʁ] next to a **back** vowel (а о у ы), [k]/[ɡ] next to a **front** vowel — with the governing vowel chosen by position: a **coda** takes the *preceding* vowel (ак→`aq`, Балык→`bɑɫɯq`), an **onset** the *following* (Баткен→`batken` — к is [k] before front е though the word also has back а). Plus **dark-l harmony** (л→ɫ back / l front), **long vowels** (doubling → Vː: тоо→`toː`), **⟨ж⟩→[d͡ʒ]** (affricate, not [ʒ]), ⟨ң⟩→[ŋ], and **intervocalic ⟨б⟩→[β]** lenition (обон→`oβon`). Turkic-decimal numbers (жыйырма бир = 21). **90.7% folded** on wikipron kir_cyrl broad (HUMAN, **only 888**, proper-noun-heavy) — **real-word 91.9%**. The folds are all one-phoneme allophony the referee writes inconsistently (ɫ~l, ɑ~a, r~ɾ, ʂ~ʃ, and the front-velar palatalisation **c~k / ɟ~ɡ** — the -лик/-лык suffix class, эстелик→`estelic`). Residual = single-letter/letter-name entries (~37, unmodelable) + foreign names (Malmö, Düsseldorf). **🔷 single-source** (small referee). **Outstanding:** a larger/2nd referee (kaikki ky) would tighten the back-⟨г⟩ ɡ~ʁ~ɢ tail. |
| **la** Latin | 92.1% (.99) | — | 🔷 | **CLASSICAL Latin (the reconstructed pronunciation, Allen *Vox Latina*) — Italic (Indo-European); Latin script with MACRONS marking length.** A dead literary/liturgical language, in scope (a vast written corpus). Classical Latin g2p is one of the fleet's most rule-regular targets — near-fully deterministic from macronized spelling. **The core is vowel QUANTITY × QUALITY:** short vowels **LAX** (⟨e i o u⟩→[ɛ ɪ ɔ ʊ]) while a macron gives length + tenseness (ā→[aː] … ū→[uː]); a short vowel in **HIATUS** stays TENSE (alia→[alia], creātūra→[kreaːtuːra] — Allen's close-in-hiatus rule). **The consonant signatures:** ⟨c⟩ is **ALWAYS [k]** (no palatalization — Caesar→[kae̯sar], the Ecclesiastical [t͡ʃ] is a different tradition), ⟨v⟩→[w] (vīta→[wiːta]), ⟨qu⟩→[kʷ] (aqua→[akʷa]), ⟨ngu⟩+V→[ŋ ɡʷ] (lingua→[lɪŋɡʷa]), ⟨x⟩→[k s], ⟨gn⟩→[ŋ n] (word-initial→[n], gnātus), the aspirates ⟨ph th ch rh⟩→[pʰ tʰ kʰ rʰ] (Greek loans); word-initial/intervocalic ⟨i j⟩→glide [j], **GEMINATE [j j] after a short vowel** (eius→[ɛjjʊs]) but single after a long one (Phīnēia→[…neːja]); **DARK ⟨l⟩→[ɫ]** (clear when geminate or before a front vowel — fīlia→[fiːlia]); ⟨b⟩→[p] before ⟨s t⟩ (absorbita→[apsɔrbɪta]); intervocalic ⟨z⟩→[z z]; ⟨n⟩→[ŋ] before a velar; and the signature **word-final ⟨-Vm⟩→ nasalized LONG vowel [Ṽː]** (bellum→[bɛllũː], aquam→[akʷãː]) — the *Vox Latina* treatment of final -m. **Penult/antepenult WEIGHT stress** (heavy penult = long/diphthong nucleus or closed syllable) is emitted (deterministic; the backbone folds ˈ). **92.1% folded / 98.9% symbol** vs wikipron lat_latn_clas_narrow (**44,907** human headwords — LARGE). Folds: intervocalic ⟨h⟩ voiced [ɦ]~[h] (the referee also drops it), the diphthong offglide diacritic U+032F. Residual = referee QUANTITY inconsistency (Casīna spelled with a macron but transcribed short; the ū̆/ī̆ "common quantity" mark is attested BOTH ways) + intervocalic ⟨h⟩-drop variants + rare Greek/Hebrew loans (ou→[uː] acoustica, Iō, mahomētāna) + in-/con- prefix assimilation (inmātūra→imm-). **🔷 human single-source-FAMILY:** wikipron Classical is large and robust, but its only corroboration is the SAME Wiktionary source (kaikki) or an authored implementation of the same Allen reconstruction — no independent human attestation, and epitran has no lat mapping. **Outstanding — deferred:** the **Ecclesiastical** (Italianate) tradition — a future `tradition` param over the same scan (⟨c⟩ before front→[t͡ʃ], ⟨ae oe⟩→[e], ⟨v⟩→[v], no vowel length, ⟨gn⟩→[ɲ]); numbers (Roman numerals + cardinals); a lexical macron-quantity source to resolve the "common quantity" tail. |
| **lb** Luxembourgish | 72.1% (.93) | — | 🔷 | **West Germanic (Moselle Franconian) — Lëtzebuergesch, the national language of Luxembourg (~390k), Latin script.** A German-derived orthography with a distinctive DIPHTHONG system + a heavy French-loan superstrate. The engine is a greedy longest-match grapheme scan + German-style code rules. **The diphthongs are the core signal** (non-obvious mappings): ⟨ei ai⟩→[ai̯], ⟨au⟩→[æu̯], ⟨ou⟩→[əu̯], ⟨éi⟩→[ei̯] (but ⟨é⟩ alone→[eː]), ⟨ie⟩→[iə], ⟨ue⟩→[uə] — the offglide diacritic folds in the backbone, so a diphthong is compared at its two vowel segments. **The German-style consonants:** ⟨w⟩→v, ⟨v⟩→f, ⟨z⟩→[t͡s], ⟨ch⟩→[χ], ⟨sch⟩→ʃ, ⟨qu⟩→[kv], initial ⟨st/sp⟩→[ʃt/ʃp], onset ⟨s⟩→[z] (Sonn→zon) but ⟨ss⟩→[s]. **The signature vowel rule: short stressed ⟨e⟩→[æ]** (Belsch→bælʃ, Decken→dækən) but reduced ⟨e⟩→[ə] (the ⟨-en⟩ ending, the ⟨ge/be/ver/er⟩ prefixes) — stress ≈ the first vowel past an unstressed prefix (the Germanic default). Plus **geminate collapse** (Flott→flot), **regressive DEVOICING** before a voiceless obstruent (Abt→apt), **final ⟨g⟩→[χ] after a vowel / [k] after a consonant** (Dag→daːχ vs Alg→alk), **⟨n⟩→[ŋ] before a velar** (Bankrott→baŋkrot), and **intervocalic g-spirantization ⟨g⟩→[ʁ]** (Lager→laʁər). **72.1% folded / 93.0% symbol** vs wikipron ltz_latn_broad (3893 human headwords); the high symbol accuracy shows most misses are 1–2-phone notation/loan diffs. **Vowel LENGTH is FOLDED** (open/closed-syllable-conditioned + doubling — a full model needs the deferred weight-based stress rule). **Measured negatives** (kept the better form): ⟨ie⟩→iː (< iə), ⟨v⟩→v (< native f), coda-r vocalization (the broad referee keeps [ər]). Residual = the **French-loan lexicon tail** (Aubergine→obərʒin, Avion→avjo — ⟨g⟩→ʒ, nasal vowels, dropped finals; unpredictable from spelling), the Romance penult-stress class, and referee ie/ue/final-r notation inconsistency. **🔷 single-source-family** (broad + narrow are the same Wiktionary scrape at two transcription depths → correlated, not independent). **Outstanding — deferred:** the weight-based stress + vowel-length model, a French-loanword lexicon, numbers, the Eifeler-Regel final-n sandhi. |
| **lg** Luganda | epitran 99.1% (.97, circular) | gold (test/luganda.test.ts) | 🔷 | **Bantu (Great Lakes, JE15) — the principal language of Uganda (~11M incl. L2), Latin orthography.** A greedy g2p + gemination + vowel-lengthening-before-prenasal. Signatures: 5 vowels with **DOUBLING = LENGTH** (aa→aː); **prenasalised consonants** as single onset units (⟨mb nd ng⟩→ᵐb ⁿd ᵑɡ) that **lengthen the preceding vowel** (buganda→buɡaːⁿda); ⟨ng'⟩→ŋ (distinct from ⟨ng⟩→ᵑɡ); ⟨ny⟩→ɲ; labialisation ⟨Cw⟩→Cʷ, preserved through a prenasal (ndw→ⁿdʷ); geminate Cː (bbiri→bːiɾi). Deliberate convention divergence: ⟨c⟩=c / ⟨j⟩=ɟ palatal STOPS (Wikipedia + a Leiden fieldwork sketch both argue plosives-not-affricates) vs epitran tʃ/dʒ (folded). **⚠ Weak referee:** epitran lug-Latn is the ONLY referee (no kaikki/wikipron lug) and is itself RULE-based, so the 99.1% is a **fidelity** measure, partly CIRCULAR (cf. uz) — the phonology is grounded independently in Wikipedia. Residual = 14 English/tech loanwords. **Outstanding — deferred:** TONE (3-way H/L/falling, lexical + unwritten) and numbers. **🔷 single-source + partly-circular.** |
| **ln** Lingala | kaikki 97.1% (.90) | gold (test/lingala.test.ts) | 🔷 | **Bantu (C30B) — a major lingua franca of the Congo (~20M native + ~20-25M L2), and the FIRST Bantu in the fleet where TONE is WRITTEN.** Authored from **Meeuwis (2020) "A Grammatical Overview of Lingála"** (Rev. & Ext. Ed.), which corrected the vowel analysis over the 1998 edition. A greedy longest-match g2p (the Chichewa pattern) + accent-based per-nucleus tone (the Yoruba pattern): PRENASALISED obstruents as single onset units (⟨mb nd ng nz⟩ → ᵐb ⁿd ᵑɡ ⁿz, homorganic), ⟨ny⟩ → ɲ; 13 consonant phonemes (no native /r/ or /h/). Because Lingala **writes tone** (acute=H, háček=rising, circumflex=falling, unmarked=L), it is *rendered* per nucleus (Chao letters) and — since kaikki carries it — **directly MEASURED, not folded** (the tonal minimal pair moto/motó → mo˩to˩ / mo˩to˥). **97.1% folded vs kaikki** (the 1 miss is an isolated letter-cluster). Meeuwis-grounded facts: Kinshasa is phonemically **5-vowel** (ɛ/ɔ merged into e/o), and there are **no diphthongs** — vowel sequences are hiatus, each a tone-bearing nucleus (mái = ma.i); no vowel harmony/length/nasalisation. **Outstanding:** the ⟨e⟩=/e/~/ɛ/ casual-spelling collapse (inherent to the 5-vowel Kinshasa orthography) + toneless-input default-L (the common casual-writing case; no tone lexicon). 🔷 single-source (small kaikki referee). |
| **lo** Lao | 97.7% (.95) | — | 🟡 | **Lao — Brahmic abugida, Thai's sibling but MORE PHONEMIC** (the Lao-PDR spelling reforms stripped most Indic etymological baggage), so a self-contained authored `lao.ts` rule g2p suffices — no Sanskrit-reading dictionary. Leading-vowel reorder (ເ ແ ໂ ໃ ໄ written before the consonant), a discontinuous-vowel pattern matcher, consonant CLASSES (high/mid/low) + an 8-way coda, ຫ-led HIGH sonorants (ຫນ→[n], ຫຼ→[l]), Cວ→uːə vowel, and the **Vientiane 5-tone system** computed from class × live/dead × length × tone-mark. **The structural crux was TONE-MARK EXTRACTION** — the marks combine ABOVE the onset but appear BEFORE the vowel signs in the character stream (ຂ່າ = ຂ+່+າ), so they must be pulled out first or they corrupt vowel-pattern matching; the segmental-residual sweep was then dominated by the **ອ/ວ onset-vs-vowel ambiguity** (both are vowel signs *and* onset consonants ʔ/ʋ), resolved with a 2-char-lookahead syllabifier (ຄົນອັງກິດ→kʰon.ʔaŋ.kit vs ອຸປະກອນ→ʔu.pa.kɔːn). **97.7% folded vs kaikki Lao (segmental); tone is VERIFIED** — 100% of single-syllable segmentally-correct words match the referee's Chao contours (the table was derived from that distribution). **Outstanding:** a loanword/Sanskrit tail that needs lexical knowledge, not rules — the ໌ (yamakkan) cancellation mark + Sanskrit codas, the kʷ labialised cluster, variable Sanskrit ສ codas ([s]~[t̚]), and English loans. Single referee (no wikipron Lao). |
| **lt** Lithuanian | 86.6% folded (.99) | wikipron (15513, human) | 🔷 | **Baltic (Indo-European — a fleet-first family; the most archaic living IE branch), ~3M, Latin.** A RULE-based g2p (the Czech pattern), not a table — Lithuanian's defining feature is pervasive **hard/soft PALATALIZATION**: a consonant → Cʲ before a front vowel ⟨e ę ė i į y⟩, the softening ⟨i⟩, ⟨j⟩, or the rising diphthong ⟨ie⟩ (Dievas→dʲiɛʋɐs), spreading leftward through a cluster — EXCEPT **velars ⟨k ɡ⟩ don't receive spread** (knyga→knʲiːɡɐ, k hard before soft nʲ; derived from the referee). Plus the **softening ⟨i⟩** (silent before a back vowel, palatalising the preceding C: čia→t͡ʃʲɛ), **⟨a⟩-fronting to ɛ** after a soft consonant (Mažeikiai→…kʲɛɪ), regressive **VOICING** assimilation (dirbti→dʲɪrʲpʲtʲɪ, b→p), and n→ŋ before velars. The ogonek nosinės ⟨ą ę į ų⟩ are just LONG (not nasal). **Scored 86.6% folded / 98.7% symbol** on the **wikipron lit_latn_narrow referee (HUMAN, 15,513 words)** — the 98.7% symbol accuracy shows the segments are essentially right; the folded number is dragged by narrow-referee NOTATION noise (the soft affricate t͡ʃʲ~tʲʃʲ, the ⟨ie⟩ glide i~j~ɪ̯, gradient palatalization on leftmost cluster consonants — all ≤2-word classes). **Caveat:** the folded number is propped ~9pp by the ɔ→o fold (the native ⟨o⟩=[oː] vs loan ⟨o⟩=[ɔ] split is unrecoverable from spelling — we emit the native default; a loanword ⟨o⟩ lexicon is the deferred fix). **STRESS is lexical + pitch-accented** (two accents, unpredictable from spelling) → not marked; the referee's ¹/² pitch marks + stress-conditioned length (ː) and vowel quality (ɑ→ɐ, æ→ɛ) are folded. **🔷 single numeric source** (wikipron only) but LARGE + the standard Lithuanian phonology (Wikipedia / Cambridge JIPA). **Deferred:** lexical stress/pitch, the native ⟨o⟩=oː vs loan ⟨o⟩=ɔ split (needs a lexicon), numbers. |
| **luo** Luo (Dholuo) | 100% folded (17w) | Tucker (1994) + gold | 🔷 | **Western Nilotic (Luo group), Latin orthography — spoken around Lake Victoria in Kenya + Tanzania (~4–5M). The FIRST Nilotic language in the fleet.** A greedy longest-match g2p (the wo/lg/ki shape) + ONE code rule (a high vowel ⟨i u⟩ before another vowel → the glide ⟨j w⟩, dhiang'→ðjaŋ), plus an input normaliser that strips tone-marked citation accents (chíeng'→chieng'). Signatures: the **DENTAL vs ALVEOLAR** contrast (⟨th dh⟩→θ ð vs ⟨t d⟩→t d — dhano→ðano, thum→θum vs kidi/adek; the referee writes the dentals as fricatives θ/ð, Tucker as dental plosives t̪/d̪ — folded, the *contrast* is the phonemic fact); **PRENASALISED** voiced stops as single units (⟨mb nd nj ng⟩→ᵐb ⁿd ⁿd͡ʒ ᵑɡ; ndalo→ⁿdalo); ⟨ng'⟩→ŋ (ng'ato→ŋato) distinct from ⟨ng⟩→ᵑɡ; ⟨ny⟩→ɲ; the palatals ⟨ch⟩→t͡ʃ (rech→ɾet͡ʃ), ⟨j⟩→d͡ʒ; ⟨r⟩→ɾ. **The 9-vowel ±ATR distinction and register TONE (H/L) are UNWRITTEN** in the orthography (no ATR trigger, no tone mark) → unrecoverable from spelling, so emitted at a **+ATR / toneless default** and folded (kelo→kelo where the referee has kɛlɔ). **100% folded (17/17)** vs the **ONLY** numeric referee — the 17-word en.wiktionary Luo IPA set (no kaikki dump, no epitran luo-Latn, no wikipron). **The referee is thin and does NOT exercise the signatures foregrounded above** — the prenasalised stops, ⟨th⟩→θ, ⟨j⟩→d͡ʒ, and the ⟨u⟩-glide have ZERO referee words (only one ⟨dh⟩→ð) — so those rest on **test/luo.test.ts + Tucker (1994) *A Grammar of Kenya Luo (Dholuo)*** + Okoth-Okombo (1982), not the 100%. **🔷 single-source** — correct on every referee word + grammar-corroborated, but thin (17w) with no independent second referee. **Outstanding — deferred (documented caps):** ±ATR harmony (unwritten — needs a lexicon, like Akan tone), register tone (H/L, lexical + unwritten), and numbers. |
| **ltg** Latgalian | 77.7% (.93) | kaikki 76.0% (.93 sym) | 🔷 | **Eastern Baltic (latgaļu volūda, ~150k, Latgale in E. Latvia) — a close sibling of Latvian; Eastern Baltic**. A self-contained scan → onset palatalization → voicing assimilation. **★★ The signature is the ⟨i⟩/⟨y⟩ soft/hard split:** the front vowel letters ⟨i ī e ē⟩ **palatalize** the preceding consonant(s) (ci→`t͡sʲi`, bet→`bʲæt`, acis→`at͡sʲis`), but **⟨y⟩→[ɨ]** — a hard central vowel Latvian lacks — does **not** (cylvāks→`t͡sɨlvaːks`, byut→`bɨut`). Palatalization is an onset-cluster rule (bazneica→`bazʲnʲæit͡sa`). Vowels ⟨a e i o u y⟩→[a æ i ɔ u ɨ], macron = long; ⟨c č š ž⟩→[t͡s t͡ʃ ʃ ʒ], ⟨dz dž tz⟩→[d͡z d͡ʒ d͡z], the written palatals ⟨ļ ņ ģ ķ ř⟩→[lʲ nʲ ɡʲ kʲ rʲ], ⟨v⟩→[w] in a coda; Baltic voicing assimilation (Latgola→`ladɡɔla`). Latgalian's **pitch accent** (level/falling/broken) is unwritten → not emitted (it folds). **77.7% folded / 93.3% symbol** on wikipron ltg_latn narrow (HUMAN, 488), corroborated by **kaikki (HUMAN, 516) at 76.0% / 93.1% symbol** — two human referees. **The high symbol accuracy (93%) is the truer signal** — the segment inventory is right; the folded number is dragged by the *narrow* referee's detail (tone/glide/length) and by **lexically-inconsistent coda palatalization** (acis keeps a *hard* final -s, but ass→`asʲsʲ` softens it — a blanket coda rule was tested and *dropped* the score −13pp, so onset-only). Folds: ⟨e⟩ ɛ~æ, vowel length ː. **🔷 single-source-family** (wikipron + kaikki both Wiktionary; no epitran ltg). **Outstanding — deferred:** the coda/lexical palatalization, the pitch accent, numbers. |
| **lv** Latvian | 83.7% (.97) | — | 🔷 | **Baltic (latviešu, ~1.5M) — the second Baltic language, sibling of Lithuanian but a SEPARATE engine.** Where Lithuanian leaves things implicit, Latvian WRITES them, so the g2p is much more direct: **palatalization is WRITTEN** (⟨ģ ķ ļ ņ⟩ → ɟ c ʎ ɲ — no contextual Cʲ-spread), **vowel LENGTH is WRITTEN** (the macron ā ē ī ū → ː, emitted not folded), and **STRESS is FIXED on the first syllable** (emitted as ˈ — predictable, unlike Lithuanian's free lexical pitch-accent). Folds: the syllable TONE (level/falling/broken, unwritten → BACKBONE-stripped), the lexical ⟨e⟩ quality [æ]~[ɛ], the glide space (j→i, w→u, v→u — ⟨v⟩ is one phoneme's [v]~[w]~[u̯], written inconsistently), ⟨h⟩=[x]~[h], first-syllable ˈ (folded vs the referee's tone). | **mi** Māori | 99.8% (1.00) | — | 🔷 | **Eastern Polynesian, New Zealand (~185k), Latin — one of the SIMPLEST orthographies in the fleet, a near-CEILING first-pass bring-up.** Māori is a near-1:1 phonemic orthography, so a direct grapheme map nails it with almost no rules: the 5 vowels + the **macron = LENGTH** (⟨ā ē ī ō ū⟩→[aː eː iː oː uː]), the simple consonants ⟨p t k m n h w⟩, ⟨r⟩→[ɾ] (tap), and the two digraphs **⟨wh⟩→[ɸ]** (whenua→ɸenua) and **⟨ng⟩→[ŋ]** (ngā→ŋaː, tangata→taŋata). Strict **CV syllables** — no codas, no clusters, no glide formation, so a vowel sequence stays fully separate (Aotearoa→aoteaɾoa, each vowel its own mora). **99.8% folded / 100.0% symbol on the first pass** vs wikipron mri_latn_broad (1005 human headwords); the only 2 "misses" are the non-Māori glyph ⟨Ḵ⟩ (a letter-name row), so real-word accuracy is effectively 100%. The 100% symbol accuracy confirms zero segmental error. Stress (mora-based, unwritten) is not emitted; the rhotic r~ɾ and ⟨wh⟩ ɸ~f (dialect) are folded. **🔷 single-source-family** (broad + narrow are the same Wiktionary scrape at two phonetic depths, correlated — no independent 2nd source), but near-ceiling on a trivially-correct map. **Outstanding — deferred:** the mora-based stress model, numbers. |
| **mk** Macedonian | 99.0% (.998) | — | 🔷 | **South Slavic (македонски, ~2M), Cyrillic — a near-CEILING bring-up on one of the fleet's LARGEST referees (63,024 human headwords).** Macedonian is fully phonemic with **no vowel reduction**, so a left-to-right grapheme scan + the shared South-Slavic phonotactics (the Bulgarian pattern) nails it on the first pass. Two Macedonian specifics: the **palatals are DISTINCT LETTERS** ⟨ѓ ќ љ њ ѕ џ ј⟩→ɟ c ʎ ɲ d͡z d͡ʒ j (no ь/я/ю palatalization machinery), and **STRESS is FIXED on the ANTEPENULT syllable** (predictable → emitted: Македонија→makɛˈdɔnija, планина→ˈpɫanina). Rules: **dark-l** (⟨л⟩→[l] before е/и/ј, [ɫ] elsewhere: волк→vɔɫk vs леб→lɛp), **syllabic ⟨р⟩→[r̩]** (прст→pr̩st, срце bears the stress), **final devoicing** (град→ɡrat, нож→nɔʃ, ѕид→d͡zit), regressive voicing, n→ŋ. **99.0% folded / 99.8% symbol** — the entire ~1% residual is single-letter/**letter-name rows** (Б→referee "bə" vs our sound "p"), not engine defects; real-word accuracy ≈100%. Numbers compose with the "и" connector (дваесет и еден). 🔷 single-source but VERY large + near-ceiling (a 2nd referee would lift it to ✅; none wired). **Outstanding:** a 2nd independent referee; clitic ("и") stress in number strings (folded). |

| **mn** Mongolian (Khalkha) | 52.5% folded (.50) | en.wiktionary mn (1342) | 🔷 | **Mongolic, Cyrillic — a FLEURS-102 language and one of the DEEPEST orthographies in the fleet.** Cyrillic Khalkha does NOT map cleanly to speech: only the first-syllable vowel is realised full; a written non-initial SHORT vowel reduces to a single reduced-vowel phoneme (ə, harmony-coloured ʊ/ɵ) or **DELETES word-finally**, with **EPENTHESIS** breaking the resulting cluster (хурга→xʊrəɢ: final а drops, ə appears in r-g). The engine is a greedy Cyrillic scan + those deep rules: the **ASPIRATED-vs-UNASPIRATED stop system** (б=[p] т=[tʰ], г=[ɡ~ɢ] к=[kʰ], ж=[t͡ʃ] ч=[t͡ʃʰ], з=[t͡s] ц=[t͡sʰ]), **л→ɮ** (voiced lateral fricative — the Mongolian signature), **local back-harmony** (г→ɢ/х→χ next to а/о/у), **final н→ŋ** (keyed on the original spelling position, so хаана→xaːn but заан→tsaːŋ), **final в→f** devoicing, doubled vowels→long, iotated е/ё/я/ю, palatalizing ь. **52.5% FOLDED vs en.wiktionary Mongolian** (1342 Cyrillic words, dropping 118 traditional-script entries). The folded % **UNDERSTATES quality** (the bn pattern): the referee is very NARROW (aspiration, half-length, syllable-dots, and the one reduced-vowel phoneme written ə~ɐ~ɪ — all folded), and the ceiling is loanword/place-name non-initial full vowels (Албани→alpan) that need a lexicon — the native common-word output is sound canonical Mongolian (Монгол→mɔŋɢʊɮ, сургууль→sʊrɢuːɮ, хот→χɔtʰ). **NEGATIVE result:** turning reduction OFF scored WORSE (40.5%), so the native reduction is both correct and higher-scoring. **Follow-ups landed:** **cardinal numbers** (absolute/attributive compositor — гурав→гурван, хорин таван мянга = 25000), a **loanword heuristic** (a word mixing back+front vowels violates harmony → it's a loanword, keep its non-initial vowels full: Герман→ɡermaŋ, +0.6pp; a full loanword *lexicon* is data-blocked — built from the only referee it would be circular memorization), and a **Mongol-bichig (traditional-script) front-end** (transliterate the classical script → Cyrillic → reuse the engine, incl. the ⟨г⟩-deletion+long-vowel contraction ᠮᠣᠩᠭᠣᠯ→монгол→mɔŋɢʊɮ; **33.1%** on a 118-word secondary referee — the ceiling is the *lexical* classical→modern sound changes). **🔷 single-source.** |
| **mos** Mooré | 94.9% folded (.90) | Wiktionary + FSI (1966) | 🔷 | **Niger-Congo GUR (Oti-Volta) — the largest language of Burkina Faso (~8M incl. L2), Latin (Burkinabé) orthography; the FIRST Gur language in the fleet.** A greedy longest-match g2p + gemination + nasal assimilation (the Wolof shape), **94.9% folded** vs en.wiktionary Moore (39 IPA words); the 2 residuals are referee artifacts (a gemination-notation inconsistency `lekolle` + a ⟨y⟩→"y" typo `yĩn-maasem`), so the segmental backbone is ~100%. **Dual-grounded**: the Wiktionary referee (modern orthography) + the **FSI Moré Basic Course** (Lehr, Redden & Balima 1966) phonology, which independently corroborates ⟨r⟩=the flap allophone of /d/, ⟨o⟩=/o/ (no ⟨ɔ⟩; /ɔ/ is the hiatus ⟨oa⟩, laloa→laloa), ⟨ny⟩=ɲ, /n/=[n,ŋ], and the H/L tone. Signatures: **dedicated ATR letters** ⟨ɛ⟩=ɛ / ⟨ɩ⟩=ɪ / ⟨ʋ⟩=ʊ; **DOUBLING = LENGTH** (aa→aː, ʋʋ→ʊː); **NASAL = TILDE** (ã ẽ ĩ õ ũ, nasal-long ⟨ãa⟩→ãː); ⟨r⟩=ɾ (tap), ⟨y⟩=j, ⟨ny⟩=ɲ, ⟨sh⟩=ʃ, ⟨ʼ⟩=ʔ; a doubled consonant → geminate Cː; **⟨n⟩→ŋ before g/k** (tenga→teŋɡa, FSI /n/=[n,ŋ]). **TONE** (2-tone H/L) is *not written* in the orthography (contextual) → not emitted; the referee's tone marks + syllable dots are folded (the Wolof-stress pattern). **Outstanding — deferred:** tone, numbers. **🔷** — one numeric referee word-list (Wiktionary, small, loanword-heavy — no kaikki dump / epitran / wikipron mos); FSI corroborates the phonology but is not a second orthography-matched referee (the ak-cites-Dolphyne pattern). |

The notable rule is **retainFinalAfterCluster** — Marathi keeps the final inherent schwa after a cluster/geminate (अन्न→ənːə) but deletes it after a single consonant, a rule Hindi lacks. **Outstanding (🟡):** medial-schwa variation, proven-lexical and (unlike Gujarati) *not* closable by a consensus lexicon — the two referees barely fold-align (15 clean pins). |
| **mt** Maltese | 93.0% (.98) | — | 🔷 | **The ONLY Semitic language written in the LATIN alphabet (Malti, ~520k) — a Siculo-Arabic core with a heavy Sicilian/Italian + English superstrate.** Unlike the Arabic/Hebrew abjads, Maltese orthography is fairly phonemic, so the engine is a greedy grapheme scan + the silent-letter rules + assimilation. Signatures: **⟨q⟩→ʔ** (the glottal stop, qalb→ʔalp), ⟨ċ⟩→t͡ʃ, ⟨ġ⟩→d͡ʒ, ⟨ħ⟩→ħ, ⟨x⟩→ʃ, and ⟨z⟩→t͡s vs ⟨ż⟩→z. The **silent-letter rules** are the Maltese signature: ⟨għ⟩ is silent word-medially (it colours/lengthens the vowel — folded) but surfaces as [ħ] word-final (biegħ→bɪħ); ⟨h⟩ is silent medially (adjacent vowels collapse: deheb→dɛp) but [ħ] word-final (fih→fɪħ); ⟨ie⟩→the long [ɪ]. Plus **final devoicing + regressive voicing assimilation** (ħobż→ħɔps, Attard→attart), **⟨n⟩→m before a labial** (ġenb→d͡ʒɛmp), word-final geminate DEGEMINATION (Ħadd→ħat; a MEDIAL geminate is kept, qattus→ʔattus), and **affricate GEMINATION** — a doubled affricate surfaces as STOP+affricate, ⟨ġġ⟩→[dd͡ʒ] not [d͡ʒd͡ʒ] (mweġġa→mwɛdd͡ʒa; the +4.7pp win). **93.0% folded / 98.2% symbol** vs wikipron mlt_latn_broad (15,837 human headwords — LARGE). **Vowel LENGTH is FOLDED** (the Estonian call): ~50% of referee lines carry ː, but Maltese length is STRESS-CONDITIONED (the Arabic-style weight system) + from the silent ⟨għ h⟩ — a full model needs the deferred weight-based stress rule, so we emit the short-vowel segment skeleton and fold length + the għ-pharyngealization. **MEASURED NEGATIVE:** devoicing-only regressed the score (regressive voicing is net-positive; Afgan→afɡan is a rare loan exception) → kept both-direction assimilation. 🔷 single-source but LARGE + real-word ≈ceiling; the residual is the ⟨għ⟩-as-glide diphthongs + loan-name spellings + referee article artifacts (all 1–2×). **Outstanding — deferred:** the weight-based stress + vowel-length model, numbers, the ⟨għ⟩-as-glide coloring. |
| **nci** Classical Nahuatl | 93.1% (.90) · 98.8% symbol | wikipron broad (human, 886) + kaikki (2329) | 🔷 | **Uto-Aztecan, nāhuatlahtōlli, the language of the Aztec Empire (16th-c. A **position-aware scan** — the Spanish-orthography context rules can't be a flat digraph table. **★ 8 vowels /a e i o/ × length** — LENGTH is UNWRITTEN in traditional texts (macron-less) → we emit SHORT vowels (the backbone strips the referee's ː). No diphthongs. ⟨u⟩ is NEVER a vowel natively (only part of cu/uc/hu/uh). **★★ THE SPANISH-ORTHOGRAPHY CONTEXT RULES (§2.4)**: **⟨c⟩→[s]** before e/i, else **[k]**; **⟨qu⟩→[k]**; **⟨z⟩→[s]**; **⟨cu⟩+V / V⟨uc⟩→[kʷ]** (labialised velar); **⟨hu⟩+V / V⟨uh⟩→[w]**; **saltillo ⟨h⟩→[ʔ]** (post-vowel only — never word-initial, §2.3.3); **⟨x⟩→ʃ**, **⟨tz tl ch⟩→[t͡s t͡ɬ t͡ʃ]**. **★ THE ⟨chu⟩ TRAP**: ⟨ch⟩ before ⟨u⟩+V is [k]-coda + ⟨hu⟩[w] (cachuah=/kakwa/), NOT the affricate. Verified on the tricky cases: teuctli→`tekʷt͡ɬi`, cuauhtli→`kʷawt͡ɬi`, cihuatl→`siwat͡ɬ`, xochitl→`ʃot͡ʃit͡ɬ`. **TWO corroborating HUMAN referees: wikipron nci_latn_broad (886) 93.1% folded / 98.8% symbol** (primary) **+ kaikki (2329) 92.1% / 98.3%** (secondary) — a strong, well-corroborated quality signal. | **nb** Norwegian Bokmål | **freq-wtd 90.7% shipped** · 63.4% rules (.20) | kaikki/wikipron + NST (indep.) | 🟢 | **North Germanic, Norway (~5M), Latin — Urban East Norwegian.** A **two-tier** phonemizer: an **NST pronunciation lexicon** (National Library of Norway, **CC0** — INDEPENDENT of the Wiktionary referee → non-circular; ~38k common forms, ~98% of real-text tokens, carrying the lexical stress + vowel quality the deep orthography underdetermines) → a **cleanroom rule g2p** fallback (`phonemizeWordRules`, the non-circular eval floor). **Read the frequency-weighted number, not the raw.** The kaikki/wikipron referee is *dictionary-shaped* (over-samples rare Latinate inflections whose non-initial lexical stress a first-syllable engine can't recover), so the raw uniform % is a sampling artifact; the **frequency-weighted metric** (`freq/nb.txt`, OpenSubtitles token counts) = **90.7% shipped** (lexicon→rules) vs **63.4% rules-only** — and NST corroborates the rule engine independently (breaking the single-source caveat). The rule core: **complementary vowel length** picks *quality* (⟨o⟩→[uː]/[ɔ], ⟨u⟩→[ʉː]/[ʉ], ⟨å⟩→[oː]/[ɔ]; bok→`buːk`, hus→`hʉːs`, norsk→`nɔʂk`); **front-vowel softening** (sk/k/g→ʃ/ç/j), digraphs sj/skj→ʃ, kj/tj→ç, hv→ʋ, word-initial hj/gj/lj/dj→j; **retroflex** r+coronal→ʈ/ɳ/ɭ/ʂ (barn→`bɑːɳ`); **silent final ⟨d⟩** (god→`ɡuː`); unstressed ⟨e⟩→schwa. **OOV tier (async):** a **stress-predicting BiLSTM** tagger (`phonemizeNbNeural`, ONNX; trained on the full 631k NST with the stress mark in the tag alphabet, so it reads stress POSITION + vowel quality directly from spelling — the deep-orthography win a first-syllable heuristic misses). On the held-out OOV split it far outstrips the averaged-perceptron prototype (56.6%) and the rule engine, filling the names/novel-compound tail; `onnxruntime-node` optional, falls back to the sync path when absent. Folds strip the pitch accent (tonelag 1/2, unwritten → deferred), the mixed rhotic (ɾ~ʁ), and the low-vowel ɑ~a. **🟢 reliable · bounded** — vowel quality/stress is now closed by the lexicon (attested) + tagger (OOV); the irreducible remainder is **pitch accent**, which the orthography does not encode (a principled cap, not a backlog item). |

This module targets **Languedocien** (the de-facto koine, the wikipron referee's core, and the most-likely FLEURS reader dialect — though the FLEURS audio dialect is **unverified**, since FLEURS is built on dialect-neutral FLoRes text). A greedy Gallo-Romance scan: the signature vowels **unstressed ⟨o⟩→[u]** (Barcelona→baɾselunɔ), **final ⟨a⟩→[ɔ]** (França→fɾansɔ), ⟨u⟩→[y]/[w], ⟨ò⟩→[ɔ]; ⟨c g⟩ softening, ⟨lh⟩→[ʎ] ⟨nh⟩→[ɲ] ⟨ch⟩→[t͡ʃ] ⟨j⟩→[d͡ʒ] ⟨v⟩→[b]; intervocalic ⟨s⟩→[z]; and — the biggest lever (+11pp) — the **Languedocien final-consonant deletion**: a word-final ⟨n r⟩ after a vowel drops (Japon→dʒapu, cantar→kanta). **70.4% folded / 93.3% symbol** vs wikipron oci_latn_broad (675 human headwords — **small + dialect-mixed**), so the score is a **folded/noisy proxy**, not a clean per-dialect validation (I fold the rhotic ʁ~ɾ, spirantization β/ð/ɣ→b/d/ɡ, ⟨ch⟩ t͡s~t͡ʃ, the final-⟨a⟩ spread). Stress is unwritten → not emitted. **🔷 thin + dialect-mixed single source** (kaikki's 1,136 IPA is Béarn/Niçard-tagged → unusable as a Languedocien gold). **Outstanding — deferred:** dialect overrides (Gascon/Provençal/Niçard) as a `dialect` param — *not attempted*, because there's no clean per-dialect data to build or validate them (guessing would be the `bho` circular-clone trap); a stress model; numbers. |

The shipped lexicon adds referee-verified readings on 6.5% of running-text tokens. | **pap** Papiamentu | 70%* (.93) | — | 🔷 | **An IBERIAN- (Portuguese/Spanish-) lexified CREOLE of the ABC islands (Aruba, Bonaire, Curaçao), ~340k — a creole**. Targets the Curaçao/Bonaire **phonemic** orthography; a greedy scan. **★ The hallmark is coda-⟨n⟩ RETENTION** (Maurer; Kouwenberg & Murray): Papiamentu **keeps** a coda ⟨n⟩ — a **word-final ⟨n⟩ → the velar nasal [ŋ]** (+ vowel nasalization: bon→`bõŋ`, federashon→`fedeɾaˈʃõŋ`, mashin→`maˈʃĩŋ`), while a ⟨n⟩ before a consonant or vowel **stays [n]** (kontra→`ˈkontɾa`, Papiamentu→`papiaˈmentu`). ★ **Review-corrected** from an initial "nasalize + *drop* the ⟨n⟩" model: the phonology reviewer showed that dropping is the Wiktionary referee's **Portuguese-style over-transcription** (`ʃõ`), not Papiamentu speech, which retains the nasal — so the **output** now retains it and the dropped-⟨n⟩ is folded (ŋ→∅). Digraphs ⟨ch sh dj zj⟩→[t͡ʃ ʃ d͡ʒ ʒ]; open-vowel letters ⟨è ò ù⟩→[ɛ ɔ ø] + the **⟨ou⟩ diphthong → [ɔu]** (Kòrsou→`ˈkɔɾsɔu`); ⟨ñ⟩→[ɲ]; degemination (Willemstad→`wilemstad`); acute ⟨á é í ó ú⟩ = irregular stress (abolí→`aboˈli`), default penult (a diphthong = one nucleus) / **ultimate** for a consonant-final word. Papiamentu's lexical **pitch-accent** (H/L, párà) is transcribed by the referee but not written → not emitted (it folds). **\*70% folded / 93.1% symbol (14/20) — on a TINY 20-word referee** (kaikki Papiamentu + Wiktionary; no wikipron/epitran pap); the % is **depressed by the referee's divergent transcription** (dropped coda-n, unmarked ⟨o⟩→[ɔ]) — the output is canonically correct (verified on goldens). We deliberately do **not** fold ⟨o⟩→[ɔ] (it would mask the real ⟨o⟩/⟨ò⟩ = /o/-/ɔ/ contrast the orthography encodes — kontra→`kɔntra` is a referee artifact, taken as a residual). Other residuals: coda-⟨r⟩→[h] (deporte→`depohte`, a Curaçao dialect feature), the referee's medial coda-n *drop* (canonical keeps it), intervocalic ⟨v⟩→[f] devoicing in -ivo (sustantivo). **🔷 thin single-source-family; the number understates the engine.** **Outstanding — deferred:** numbers, a larger referee, the coda-r→h / pitch-accent lexica. |
| **pcm** Nigerian Pidgin | single-source (grammars) | gold (test/naija.test.ts) | 🔷 | **First English-lexified creole; gold-anchored (Faraclas 1996 + the NLA manual).** Targets the media (English-etymological, BBC-Pidgin) orthography people actually write. Engine: a ~235-word lexicon for irregular media spellings + a Naija rule g2p (7 vowels, TH-stopping, labial-velars, no reduction). The neat trick: an English-spelled known word is *nativised* (its CMUdict IPA mapped to the 7-vowel system, non-rhotic), while substrate loans (OOV in the English dict) route through the phonemic rules — dict-membership is the routing signal. **Normalization (done):** the symbol tier + number shapes, words sourced from the mined pcm corpus except where noted — `percent` (the corpus has 27 `%` but only ONE instance of the word, so it is sourced from pcm.wikipedia: "85 percent", "reduce by about 0.87 percent"), currency ⟨dolla⟩ (×2, against English ⟨dollar⟩ ×1 — the English spelling is the minority form) and ⟨naira⟩ (×5), `&`→⟨an⟩ (×24), plus a `US$` compound key (×6; the code is orthographic and goes unvoiced, since the nativise reads ⟨US⟩ as the pronoun *us*). The number class was a bare `\d+`, so the thousands comma and decimal dot were claimed by the clause branch — `₦2,000` read *tu , ziɾo* ("two [pause] zero") and `3.5` *tɾi . faiv*; both shapes are the corpus norm (60 grouped, 61 decimals), and the decimal word is ⟨point⟩ (×3), read digit-by-digit. **Outstanding:** single-source (🔷); tone (unmarked → underivable) deferred, and the nativise inherits GenAm vowel merges. **ORDINALS** (×26) read as ordinals — the productive rule is **`nɔmba` + the CARDINAL** (APiCS survey 17: *nomba wo̱n ~ fe̱st, no̱mba tu ~ se̱kond, no̱mba tri ~ te̱d, no̱mba for …*), with suppletive fɛst/sɛkond/tɛd for 1–3; that rule is what made 4th+ expressible at all, since an English-style table would be unsourced above ⟨third⟩. Corroborated in-corpus: ⟨nomba⟩ ×18 vs English ⟨number⟩ ×12, ⟨fest⟩ ×47 / ⟨sekond⟩ ×21 vs ⟨first⟩ ×12 / ⟨second⟩ ×5. ⚠ Vowels follow APiCS where the media spelling underspecifies them (the ⟨e̱⟩/⟨o̱⟩ underdot is /ɛ/,/ɔ/). **UNITS** km→⟨kilomita⟩, m→⟨mita⟩ — the mined corpus has 29 unit-bearing numbers and **zero** spelled-out unit words, so these came from pcm.wikipedia ("85 kilomita fom di main kampos", "di 100 mita race"); kg/cm return zero hits there and stay undeclared rather than extrapolated, since the nativise reads English ⟨kilometer⟩ as *kalamata*. **`Dr.`** → ⟨Doctor⟩ (×3 in corpus) — it had been nativised as a word to *dɾaiv* ("drive"); dot optional, and consumed so it does not read as a pause. **INITIALISMS** are letter-spellings unless lexified — `FC` scanned as a word gave the unpronounceable *fk* and `A.I.` split on the dots so ⟨I⟩ hit the pcm PRONOUN lexicon (*a*); the lexified test is the English dict, which already carries both the word-pronounced acronyms (FIFA, NEPA) and the conventionally-spelled-out ones (APC ˈeᶦpʰˈiːsˈiː, BBC, TV, CD), so only a dict MISS is spelled out — rescuing FC, LGA, PSV, YBNL. ⚠ `letterNames` is declared because the dict cannot supply it: its single-letter entries are the WORDS, so ⟨a⟩ resolves to the article [ə], not the letter name [e]. ⚠ A DICT MISS IS NOT SUFFICIENT — the run must also contain **no vowel letter**. Gating on the dict alone spelled out 87 of the corpus's 133 all-caps types; most were wins, but eight were word-acronyms the base engine already read correctly (COVID → *si o vi ai di*, plus ABIA, BOGA, AFCON, APGA, INEC, AIBA, NADECO). The vowel test keeps every consonant-only win and rescues all eight. ⚠ Its cost, stated: a vowel-bearing letter-acronym (LGA, PGA, AG, UEFA) keeps reading as a word — unchanged from the base engine, so a smaller win rather than a regression. **TIMES** follow the English pattern with pcm number words (`5:30`→*faiv tɔti*, `5:00`→bare hour, `00:05`→*ziɾo ziɾo faiv*, keeping the zero rather than borrowing English's unsourced "oh") — ⚠ **PROVISIONAL**: no pcm clock-reading source was found, and the one phrasebook hit uses an orthography (*twu/tiri/sevun*) that contradicts this engine's own numerals. **Outstanding — deferred:** `Mrs.` and other titles (unsourced); `kg`/`cm` (zero attestations). |

| **pt-BR** Portuguese (Brazilian) | 86.9% (.86) | gold 100% (78) | ✅ | **The SECOND ACCENT VARIANT — "neutral"/paulistano BP** over the European `pt` engine, and the first that needed **engine parameterization** rather than a post-process (`dialect:"bp"`): EP pretonic vowel reduction is not recoverable from EP surface forms, so the delta lives inside the engine. Delta: position-split reduction (only FINAL atonic raises e→i/o→u; pretonic stays MID — bonito→bonitu NOT the EP bunitu), coda-s alveolar s/z (estado→estadu), /t d/→t͡ʃ/d͡ʒ before [i] and the onset glide (tia→t͡ʃia, adiado→ad͡ʒjadu), coda-l→[w] (sal→saw), -em→[ẽj̃] (tem→tẽj̃), stressed mid CLOSING before a nasal (abandona→abɐ̃donɐ; acute ó/é stay open), unstressed ⟨e⟩ close before coda-l (túnel→tunew). Rule-only 86.9% (non-circular); the **shipped** path adds a 1369-entry BP open/close override lexicon (rule-unpredictable stressed-vowel openness — cheque [ɛ] — mined where the rule matches no variant) → **89.3%**. **This is the locale that labels the pt training audio (FLEURS `pt_br`), so its quality — not pt-PT's — is what the model consumes.** ✅ after Runs 3–5: gold 100%, and every remaining systematic residual is INHERITED from the ✅ `pt` engine (sc-cluster absceder→abssedeɾ, güe/gue u-glide aguei, -mente pretonic, EP -ct-) — no pt-BR-specific gap; the rest is diffuse loans / Tupí regionalisms / rhotic-variant referee noise. | **si** Sinhala | 94.4% (.90) | — | ✅ | Sinhala abugida, **with text normalization** (si.wikipedia dump, 191,335 paragraphs). **The layer's headline is not a symbol class at all:** the word token `[඀-෿]+` excluded U+200D, so every ZWJ conjunct was TWO words — `ශ්‍රී` read *s rˈiː*, `ප්‍රතිශතය` *p rˈat̪isˌət̪əjə* — and the artifact's `zero-width` cell (144,214, its largest) was measuring the ordinary rakaransaya/yansaya, not markup. Stripping the joiner is phonemically lossless in Sinhala and re-joins the token; **381 of 447 corpus utterances change**. Sinhala puts every MEASURE WORD BEFORE its number (`වර්ග කිලෝමීටර් 1,001,450`, `සියයට 4.8 ක`, `පැයට කිලෝමීටර 250`), so `percentPrefix`/`currencyPrefix`/`unitPrefix` are all set and the rate is local (dative denominator, trap 47 case 1). The decimal point and the grouping comma were CLAUSE PAUSES (`12.5` → *d̪ˈoləhə . pˈahə*); `සෙල්සියස් අංශක 38 (ෆැරන්හයිට් අංශක 100.4)` is the wiki's own frame for the degree sign, and **U+2070 stands in for `°` 6/6 times**. DROP 85→33, all 33 declared in `defects.ts` with their measurements. **A manifest gap fell out of it:** `ං` before a SIBILANT fell to the ම් default, which the referee refutes (පංසු = `p ə ŋ s u`) — fixed, 93.5→93.7%. **A second phonology finding, settled by SOURCES + a four-way control:** the engine shipped ශ→s with ෂ→ʃ, which is an analysis NO description holds — [r12a's script notes](https://r12a.github.io/scripts/sinh/si.html) give both miśra letters as /ʃ/, [Wikipedia's phonology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sinhala_language) says /ʃ/ is a learned-borrowing phoneme "commonly … replaced by /s/", and both agree ශ and ෂ are the SAME sound. Measured: `ශ=s ෂ=ʃ` 607 (shipped) · `ʃ ʃ` 604 (literary, the worst) · **`s s` 612 (colloquial, chosen)** · `ʃ s` 609. The referee's own words say why — ෂ is `s` in 9/9 and ශ in 6/9, including `ශ්‍රී → s r iː`, the very word cited as THE example of Sinhala /ʃ/. **93.5% → 94.4%, symbol 98.3% → 98.6%.** **Outstanding:** ranges, the clock colon, fractions and `=`/`×` are declined WITH counts (Sinhala writes its own range connective `අතර`; every mined `=` is a glossary separator and every `×` a dimension cross); si now emits no `ʃ` at all, which is right for a spoken-Sinhala target and would be wrong for a literary one — the eval's standing `secondary-source gap` (epitran `sin`) is still the thing that would close it independently. |

| **skr** Saraiki | 75.1%* (.72) | — | 🔷 | **Lahnda (Greater Punjabi, ~26M), extended Shahmukhi abjad — the NON-tonal sibling of Punjabi.** Where Punjabi (`pa`/`pnb`) turned its historical voiced aspirates into TONE, Saraiki kept them as segments **and** retains the four **IMPLOSIVES** (ٻ→ɓ, ڄ→ʄ, ڳ→ɠ, and the retroflex ݙ→ɗ — a letter *coined in 2002* specifically to differ from Sindhi's ڏ). So it reuses the shared Lahnda machinery via `makeNativePunjabi({saraiki:true})` — the Shahmukhi front-end, gemination→length, homorganic nasal assimilation, weight stress — with **one declarative flag** toggling off the three tonal/orthographic-ambiguity behaviors: tonogenesis, the aspirated-sonorant strip (Saraiki **keeps** لھ→lʰ), and the plain-ن→ɳ infinitive heuristic (Saraiki writes ɳ explicitly as ݨ). The implosive letters live in the shared `shahmukhi.jsonc` (Punjabi text never uses them → `pa`/`pnb` are **byte-identical**). Referee: **wikipron skr_arab broad** (HUMAN, Wiktionary, 348, vowel-ful) → **🔷 single-source** (one Wiktionary tradition; a genuine independent referee, so NOT a bho-style ⛔ clone). **Outstanding:** the unwritten short-vowel + gemination tail (the abjad wall, as `pnb`/`ur`/`sd`); smaller documented classes — hamza-ya (ائی→ɛː) over-segmentation, word-final ہ, nasal-place referee-noise. A short-vowel restoration lexicon (the pnb/ur path) + numbers are deferred. |

Slovene-specific rules, derived empirically from wikipron slv_latn_broad (5,177 headwords, variants merged): **⟨lj⟩/⟨nj⟩** → l+j / n+j before a vowel (polje→pɔljɛ) but the j DROPS in coda/final (kralj→kral, konj→kɔn — not the Serbo-Croatian palatal ʎ/ɲ); **syllabic ⟨r⟩→ər** (prst→pərst, čmrlj→t͡ʃmərl); **final devoicing + regressive voicing assimilation** (grad→ɡrat, glasba→ɡlazba), with **⟨h⟩=[x] voicing-neutral** (Abhazija→abxazija, vzhod→ʋzxɔt); **⟨v⟩/⟨w⟩→ʋ** (sonorant-inert); ⟨ć/đ⟩→t͡ʃ/d͡ʒ, ⟨q/y⟩→k/i (loans). **The vowel axis is heavily FOLDED** — Slovene orthography underspecifies quality (stressed close [eː oː] vs unstressed open [ɛ ɔ]), length, the tonemic **pitch accent** (àáâ, BACKBONE-stripped), and the morphological **schwa** [ə]; all are stress/lexically conditioned and unwritten → we emit the open ɛ/ɔ default + no stress + only the syllabic-r schwa, and fold the rest. So the measured signal is the CONSONANT skeleton + the vocalization/voicing rules (the 99.4% symbol accuracy is on that same folded backbone — it does not vouch for the folded vowel axis). **l-vocalization DEFERRED (measured):** the native coda-⟨l⟩→[w] (bel→bɛʋ) is LEXICAL — a blanket rule was **net -2pp** (the name-heavy referee has ~233 kept-[l] loans/names vs ~101 vocalized natives), so ⟨l⟩=[l] and vocalization awaits a lexicon (the rule-then-lexicon discipline; note the frequent -l past participles DO vocalize but are under-sampled in a name-heavy dict). Consonants: ⟨ç⟩→t͡ʃ, ⟨j⟩→d͡ʒ, ⟨ž⟩→ʒ, ⟨ş⟩→ʃ, ⟨ň⟩→ŋ, **⟨ý⟩→j** (the glide, vs the vowel ⟨y⟩), ⟨h⟩→x, ⟨w⟩→w, ⟨r⟩→ɾ. Word-final (oxytone) stress. **THREE referees**: **kaikki Turkmen (HUMAN, Wiktionary, 530) — 80.4% folded / 94.9% symbol** (primary); **wikipron tuk_latn_broad (HUMAN, 424) — 91.5% / 97.9%** (the richest, shows length + spirantization); **epitran tuk-Latn (INDEPENDENT rule-based) — 77.0% / 95.1%**. The epitran gap IS the interdentals — it is NAIVE (keeps s/z), so we **CORRECT it** (the Shona pattern) and do NOT fold θ→s. Folds (allophonic, referee-inconsistent — not modelled): the ⟨g⟩ **spirantization/backing [ɣ ʁ ɢ]→ɡ** (Gazak→ɣɑðɑk — /ɡ/ has no fricative/stop contrast), ⟨h⟩ h~x~χ, ⟨w⟩ β~w, dark-l ɫ~l, ʊ~u, r~ɾ, ⟨e⟩ ɛ~e, ⟨o⟩ ɔ~o, ⟨ö⟩ œ~ø. **Outstanding — deferred:** the **UNWRITTEN phonemic vowel LENGTH** (Turkmen contrasts it but the spelling omits it → needs a lexicon, the folded axis), loan sibilants (some loans keep [s]), the fine g-spirantization conditioning, numbers. **🔷** kaikki human primary + wikipron (correlated) + epitran (independent, naive-corrected). |

Shipped 93.1% closes three per-word tails via lexicons (final-ʔ, unwritten stress, foreign-segment loanwords). **Outstanding — none (🟢, capped):** the remaining unreliability is an OOV tail on *unwritten native contrasts* — the final-ʔ minimal pair *bata* child [bataʔ] vs robe [bata] and unmarked stress are under-determined by the orthography. Times PROVISIONAL cardinals (alas- has zero attestation). |

Re-run against the independent CLE corpus with CLE held out, the tagger **does** generalise: **+8.7pp over the always-ə prior on unseen words**, and the old 9,016-pair Hindi-derived "gold" pool scores **+0.6pp** on that referee (docs/investigations/ur_tagger_investigation.md Runs 12–18). Not 🟢 because the only source at the required label quality is **non-commercial**, so neither it nor a model trained on it can ship — a licensing block, no longer a knowledge one. A CC-BY-SA lexicon tier was sized and declined at **+1.3pp** frequency-weighted. |

| **za** Zhuang | 99.5% (.97) | kaikki 99.5% | 🔷 | **Tai-Kadai (Kra-Dai) — Standard Zhuang (Wuming), the 1982 Latin orthography; a fresh family.** A cleanroom rule g2p with a **one-syllable-at-a-time scan**: the **pinyin-style voicing convention** (⟨b d g⟩ = the *unaspirated* /p t k/, ⟨p t k⟩ = aspirated), the **implosives ⟨mb⟩→[ɓ]/⟨nd⟩→[ɗ]**, the fricatives ⟨s⟩→[θ]/⟨r⟩→[ɣ]/⟨c⟩→[ɕ]/⟨v⟩→[β], and Zhuang's **syllable-final tone letters** (⟨z j x q h⟩ = tones 2–6, none = tone 1, a p/t/k coda = a checked tone) rendered as Chao contours. Three orthographic subtleties: ⟨e⟩ is a **silent nucleus-marker after a high vowel** (cieng→[ɕiːŋ], suen→[θuːn]); **⟨ae⟩→[ai]** open (bae→[pai]) but short **[a] before a real coda** (caet→[ɕat]); coda stops are plain. The syllabifier (the review lever, 97→99.5%) does **coda-first** (a coda-capable C before a non-vowel closes the syllable, so ⟨nd⟩ in *Yin\|du* splits n.d rather than misreading an implosive ɗ; a single C before a vowel stays the next onset by maximal onset — *Cinanz*→[ɕi.nan]), **VV-hiatus** split on a non-high 2nd vowel (*boad*→[po.ʔat]), the **[ʔ] onset** on every onset-less vowel syllable (word-initial, post-tone/coda, hiatus), and **⟨'⟩ as an explicit syllable boundary** (*Sih'anh*→[θi.ʔan]). Both referees are **Wiktionary-derived (same-source)** → 🔷 single-source, but a clean ~99.5% with no load-bearing segmental fold. **The eval is SEGMENTAL** — the backbone strips the Chao tone letters + length, so tones (deterministic from the orthographic tone letters) are rendered but not measured. **Outstanding:** ~8 residual words are **morpheme-boundary** syllabifications not recoverable from spelling (*mak+it*→[mak.ʔit], *saw+ndip*→[saɯ.ɗip] need a morpheme lexicon); plus a measured tone-accuracy check + the finer checked-tone height split. **SECOND SCRIPT — Sawndip (古壮字):** za now also reads the Han-derived LOGOGRAPHIC script via a glyph→Standard-Zhuang-reading dictionary (2412 single-codepoint glyphs mined from kaikki) routed through this same Latin g2p — **100% self-consistent** (za(reading) reproduces the glyph's kaikki IPA, segmental). This is 🔷 **reference-parity / covered-subset / DEFAULT-reading**: the polyphonic ~8% of glyphs (mostly tonal variants of one root, 那→na/naj/nax/naz) take a DEFAULT reading chosen by a salience proxy (most senses — NOT measured corpus frequency, which we lack; so it can miss the iconic reading, 那→naj not naz) with NO context disambiguation — Sawndip has no labelled running-text corpus to train one (unlike the cmn homograph case, a separate effort); OOV/unencoded (PUA / Ideographic-Description) glyphs are dropped. Wiktionary's lemma-orientation under-samples phonetic borrowings, so real-text ambiguity exceeds the measured ~8%. + za_sawndip_investigation.md. |
| **zu** Zulu | 100% (.99) | — | ✅ | Clicks/implosives/ejectives/laterals all corroborated. Done. |

## What "outstanding work" concretely means

- **🟠 scope gaps** — a whole *subsystem* deferred (a Hanzi front-end, pitch accent, compound segmentation,
  abjad short-vowel restoration) — are now **all built: no language currently sits at 🟠.** Burmese `my` was the
  last to clear it (🟠→🟡→✅: subsystems, then the rule-based segmental core, then a pronunciation lexicon for the
  lexical tail). The pattern, when a gap recurs, is to feed the engine its supported input and treat the deferred
  layer as absent (not wrong) until built.
- **🟡 lexical tails** are bounded: a specific, enumerable class (mid-vowel height, loanword C-hardening, learned
  stress) that a small exception lexicon closes, while the rule engine is right for everything else. The
  referee-gated lexicon is the template for building these when they're worth it.
- **🔷 single-source** and **⛔ cannot-verify** are *evidence* verdicts, not quality ones: the output may be fine,
  but there's only one authoritative source (🔷) or none at all (⛔), so a systematic error could hide. Most of
  these are distinct-but-under-attested languages (the Sinitic dialect stubs, the Hindi-belt clones).
- **✅** languages have no subsystem gap and no systematic residual class — only notation folds, referee noise, or
  a fold ceiling. The low-numbered ✅s (en, ga, ta, ff, vi, ko) are the ones whose referee is the limiting factor,
  *not* the engine; their real correctness anchor is the hand-authored `test/<lang>.test.ts`.

## Maintaining this doc

Not auto-generated (the verdicts are judgment calls). Refresh a row when a bring-up run lands: re-run
`npx tsx tools/referee-eval/eval.ts <code>` for the measured %, read its **residual classes** (systematic → real
work; diffuse → referee noise). The referee % alone is
never the maturity — always pair it with the residual composition and the unit test.
